**Albania**,[^cite_note-10] officially the **Republic of Albania**,[^cite_note-11] is a country in [Southeastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeastern_Europe). It is located in the [Balkans](4829.md), on the [Adriatic](23275478.md) and [Ionian Seas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionian_Sea) within the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md), and shares land borders with [Montenegro](20760.md) to the northwest, [Kosovo](17391.md) to the northeast, [North Macedonia](23564616.md) to the east and [Greece](12108.md) to the south. With an area of 28,748 km<sup>2</sup> (11,100 sq mi), it has a varied range of climatic, geological, hydrological and morphological conditions. Landscapes range from rugged snow-capped mountains in the [Albanian Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accursed_Mountains) and the [Korab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korab), [Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Mountain_Range,_Albania#Skanderbeg_Mountains), [Pindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindus) and [Ceraunian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceraunian_Mountains), to fertile lowland plains extending from the [Adriatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Adriatic_Sea_Coast) and [Ionian sea coasts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Ionian_Sea_Coast). [Tirana](31075.md) is the capital and largest city, followed by [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs), [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB), and [Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shkod%C3%ABr).&#x20;

Albania was inhabited by several [Illyrian tribes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Illyrian_peoples_and_tribes), among them the [Ardiaei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ardiaei), [Bylliones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bylliones), [Dassaretii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dassaretii), [Enchele](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enchele), and [Taulantians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taulantii), with the [Chaonians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaonians) settled in the southwest. Several colonies were founded by the Ancient Greeks along the Albanian coast, most notably [Apollonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_\(Illyria\)). The [Illyrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrian_kingdom) were the dominant power before the [rise of Macedon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expansion_of_Macedonia_under_Philip_II).[^cite_note-12] Following the [Illyrian Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrian_Wars), Albania was integrated into the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) and remained in the [Byzantine Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire) after [its partition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_the_Western_Roman_Empire). During the Middle Ages, several [Albanian principalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_principalities) emerged, most notably the [Principality of Arbanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Arbanon), [Kingdom of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Albania_\(medieval\)), [Principality of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Albania_\(medieval\)) and [Albania Veneta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania_Veneta). In the 15th century, Albania became a center of resistance against [Ottoman expansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_wars_in_Europe) under the leadership of [Gjergj Kastrioti Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg), whose military campaigns repelled Ottoman advances for over two decades. Although incorporated into the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md), Albania retained distinct cultural and social identities throughout four centuries of foreign rule, culminating in the [Albanian Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_National_Awakening) in the 19th century. Albania [declared independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Declaration_of_Independence) in 1912, followed by a turbulent 20th century marked by [monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchy), foreign occupation during both World Wars, and a [communist regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Socialist_Republic_of_Albania) under [Enver Hoxha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enver_Hoxha).[^cite_note-zolo2002-13]&#x20;

Since its independence in 1912, Albania has undergone many political changes, transitioning from a monarchy to a communist regime before becoming a [sovereign](23604120.md) [parliamentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_republic) [constitutional republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_republic). Governed by a [constitution](7878011.md) prioritising the separation of powers, the political structure includes a [parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Albania), a ceremonial [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Albania), a functional [prime minister](259136.md) and a hierarchy of courts. Albania is a [developing country](78449.md) with an [upper-middle income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GNI_\(nominal,_Atlas_method\)_per_capita#Upper-middle-income_group) [economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Albania) driven by the service sector, with manufacturing and [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Albania)—which attracted over 11 million visitors in 2024—playing significant roles.[^cite_note-14] The [illegal drug trade in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegal_drug_trade_in_Albania) contributes around US\$100 million to the economy per year.[^cite_note-15] After the dissolution of its communist system, the country shifted from [centralised planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_economy) to an [open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_market) [market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_economy). Citizens have [universal health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_health_care) access and free primary and secondary education. The country is an official candidate for membership in the European Union and has been [negotiating accession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Albania_to_the_European_Union) since 2022.&#x20;

## Etymology

Main article: [Names of the Albanians and Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_the_Albanians_and_Albania)

The origin of the name "Albania" can be traced back to [medieval Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medieval_Latin) and the association with the [Illyrian tribe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ancient_tribes_in_Illyria) of the [Albani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanoi). This connection is further supported by the writings of Ancient Greek geographer [Ptolemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ptolemy) during the 2nd century AD, where he included the settlement of [Albanopolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanopolis) situated to the northeast of [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs).[^cite_note-16][^cite_note-17] The presence of a medieval settlement named [Albanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Arbanon) or Arbanon hints at the possibility of historical continuity. The relationship between these historical references and the question of whether Albanopolis was synonymous with Albanon remain subjects of scholarly debate.[^cite_note-18]&#x20;

Byzantine historian [Michael Attaleiates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Attaleiates), in his 11th-century historical account, provides the earliest undisputed reference to the Albanians, when he mentions them as participants in a revolt against [Constantinople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantinople) in 1079.[^cite_note-MadgearuGordon25b-19] He also identifies the Arbanitai as subjects of the [Duke of Dyrrachium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke_of_Durazzo_\(title\)).[^cite_note-20] In the Middle Ages, Albania was denoted as *Arbëri* or *Arbëni* by its inhabitants, who identified themselves as *Arbëreshë* or *Arbëneshë*.[^cite_note-21]&#x20;

Albanians employ the terms *Shqipëri* or *Shqipëria* for their nation, designations that trace their historical origins to the 14th century.[^cite_note-Matasović-22] But only in the late 17th and early 18th centuries did these terms gradually supersede *Arbëria* and *Arbëreshë* among Albanians.[^cite_note-Matasović-22][^cite_note-Lloshi-23] These two expressions are widely interpreted to symbolise "Children of the Eagles" and "Land of the Eagles".[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25]&#x20;

## History

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Albanian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Albanian_history).

Main article: [History of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Albania)

### Prehistory

Main article: [Prehistory of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistoric_Albania)



The remains of the [Kamenica Tumulus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamenica_Tumulus) in the [county of Korçë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_of_Kor%C3%A7%C3%AB)

[Mesolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesolithic) habitation in Albania has been evidenced in several open-air sites which were close to the [Adriatic coastline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Adriatic_Sea_Coast) and in caves. Mesolithic objects found in a cave near [Xarrë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xarr%C3%AB) include [flint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flint) and [jasper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasper) objects along with fossilised animal bones, while discoveries at Mount [Dajti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dajti) comprise bone and stone tools similar to those of the [Aurignacian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurignacian) culture.[^cite_note-Prendi-26] The [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) era began around 7000 BC and is evidenced in finds which indicate domestication of sheep and goats and small-scale agriculture. A part of the Neolithic population may have been the same as the Mesolithic population of the southern [Balkans](4829.md), like in the [Konispol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konispol) cave, where the Mesolithic stratum co-exists with Pre-Pottery Neolithic finds. [Cardium pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardium_pottery) culture appears in coastal Albania and across the [Adriatic](23275478.md) after 6500 BC, while the settlements of the interior took part in the processes that formed the [Starčevo culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star%C4%8Devo_culture).[^cite_note-27] The [bitumen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitumen) mines of [Selenicë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selenic%C3%AB) provide early evidence of bitumen exploitation in Europe, dating to Late Neolithic (from 5000 BC), when local communities used it as pigment for ceramic decoration, waterproofing, and adhesive for repairing broken vessels. The bitumen of Selenicë circulated towards eastward from the early 5th millennium BC. First evidence of its overseas trade export comes from the Neolithic and Bronze Age southern Italy. The high-quality bitumen of Selenicë has been exploited throughout all the historical ages since the Late Neolithic era until today.[^cite_note-28]&#x20;

[Indo-European migrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indo-European_migrations) in the western Balkans began after 2800 BC. The presence of Early Bronze Age [tumuli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tumulus) in the vicinity of [Apollonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_\(Illyria\)) dates to 2679±174 calBC (2852–2505 calBC). These burial mounds belong to the southern expression of the Adriatic-Ljubljana culture (related to the later [Cetina culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cetina_culture)), which moved southwards along the Adriatic from the northern Balkans. The same community built similar mounds in [Rakića Kuće](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raki%C4%87a_Ku%C4%87e), Montenegro and Shtoj, Albania.[^cite_note-29] The first [archaeogenetic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeogenetics) find related to Indo-Europeans involves a man with predominantly [Yamnaya ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Steppe_Herders) buried in a tumulus of northeastern Albania, which dates to 2663–2472 calBC.[^cite_note-30] During the Middle Bronze Age, Cetina culture sites and finds appear in Albania. Cetina culture moved southwards across the Adriatic from the [Cetina valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cetina) of [Dalmatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalmatia). Cetina finds are concentrated around southern [Lake Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Shkod%C3%ABr) and appear typically in tumulus cemeteries, such as in Shkrel and Shtoj, and hillforts like Gajtan (Shkodër), as well as cave sites like Blaz, Nezir, and Keputa (central Albania) and lake basin sites like Sovjan (southeastern Albania).[^cite_note-31]&#x20;

### Antiquity

Main article: [Antiquity in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Albania#Antiquity)



Founded in the 4th century BC, the city of [Scodra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scodra) was the capital of the [Illyrian kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrian_kingdom) of [Ardiaei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ardiaei) and [Labeatae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labeatae).

The territory was historically inhabited by [Indo-European peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-Indo-Europeans), amongst them numerous Illyrian and [Epirote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epirus_\(ancient_state\)) tribes. There were also several [Greek colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_colonisation). The territory referred to as [Illyria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyria) corresponded roughly to the area east of the Adriatic Sea in the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md), extending in the south to the mouth of the [Vjosë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vjosa).[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33] The first account of the Illyrian groups comes from the [Periplus of the Euxine Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Periplus_of_the_Euxine_Sea), a Greek text written in the 4th century BC.[^cite_note-wilkes-34] The [Bryges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryges) were also present in central Albania, while the south was inhabited by the Epirote [Chaonians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaonians), whose capital was at [Phoenice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenice).[^cite_note-wilkes-34][^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36] Other colonies, such as [Apollonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_\(Illyria\)) and [Epidamnos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epidamnos), were established by Greek city-states on the coast by the 7th century BC.[^cite_note-wilkes-34][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38]&#x20;



[Apollonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_\(Illyria\)) was an important [Greek colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colonies_in_antiquity#Greek_colonies) on the [Illyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyria) coast along the [Adriatic Sea](23275478.md) and one of the western points of the [Via Egnatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Via_Egnatia) route, which connected Rome and [Constantinople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantinople).

The Illyrian [Taulanti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taulantii) were a powerful Illyrian tribe among the earliest recorded tribes in the area. Taulanti ruler [Glaucias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glaucias_of_Taulantii) and [Dardanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dardanians_\(Trojan\)) ruler [Cleitus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleitus_\(son_of_Bardylis\)) fought against [Alexander the Great](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_the_Great) at the [Battle of Pelium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Pelium) in 335 BC. [Cassander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cassander) of [Macedon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonia_\(ancient_kingdom\)), captured [Apollonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonia_\(Illyria\)) and crossed the river [Genusus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shkumbin) ([Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language): *Shkumbin*) in 314 BC. A few years later, Glaucias laid siege to Apollonia and captured the Greek colony of [Epidamnos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epidamnos).[^cite_note-39]&#x20;

The Illyrian [Ardiaei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ardiaei) tribe, centred in Montenegro, ruled over most of the territory of northern Albania. Their kingdom reached its greatest extent under [King Agron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agron_of_Illyria), the son of [Pleuratus II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleuratus_II). Agron extended his rule over other neighbouring tribes as well.[^cite_note-40] Following Agron's death in 230 BC, his wife, [Teuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teuta), inherited the kingdom. Teuta's forces extended their operations further southwards to the Ionian Sea.[^cite_note-41] In 229 BC, Rome declared war[^cite_note-42] on the kingdom for extensively plundering Roman ships. The war ended in an Illyrian defeat in 227 BC. Teuta was succeeded by [Gentius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentius) in 181 BC.[^cite_note-43] Gentius clashed with the Romans in 168 BC, initiating the [Third Illyrian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Illyrian_War). The conflict resulted in the Roman conquest of the region by 167 BC. The Romans split the region into three administrative divisions.[^cite_note-44]&#x20;

### Middle Ages

Main article: [Albania in the Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania_in_the_Middle_Ages)



The town of [Krujë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kruj%C3%AB) was the capital of the [Principality of Arbanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Arbanon) in the Middle Ages.

The [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) was split in 395 upon the death of [Theodosius I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodosius_I) into an [Eastern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Roman_Empire) and [Western Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Roman_Empire), in part because of the increasing pressure from threats during the [Barbarian Invasions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migration_Period). From the 6th century into the 7th century, the [Slavs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Slavs) crossed the [Danube](21209639.md) and largely absorbed the indigenous Greeks, Illyrians, and [Thracians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thracians) in the [Balkans](4829.md); thus, the Illyrians were mentioned for the last time in historical records in the 7th century.[^cite_note-BideleuxJeffries2007-45][^cite_note-Schaefer2008-46]&#x20;

In the 11th century, the [Great Schism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East%E2%80%93West_Schism) formalised the break of communion between the [Eastern Orthodox](10186.md) and [Western Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church), which is reflected in Albania through the emergence of a Catholic north and Orthodox south. The Albanian people inhabited the west of [Lake Ochrida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ochrida) and the upper valley of the River Shkumbin and established the [Principality of Arbanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Arbanon) in 1190 under the leadership of [Progon of Kruja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progon,_Lord_of_Kruja).[^cite_note-47] The realm was succeeded by his sons [Gjin Progoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjin_Progoni) and [Demetrio Progoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demetrio_Progoni).&#x20;

Upon the death of Dhimiter, the territory came under the rule of the Albanian-Greek [Gregorios Kamonas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorios_Kamonas) and subsequently under the [Golem of Kruja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golem_of_Kruja).[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-Abulafia-49][^cite_note-Genealogist-50] In the 13th century, the principality was dissolved.[^cite_note-Clements31-51][^cite_note-PickardÇeliku2008-52][^cite_note-Norris1993-53] Arbanon is considered to be the first sketch of an Albanian state, which retained a [semi-autonomous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-autonomous) status as the western extremity of the [Byzantine Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Empire), under the Byzantine [Doukai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doukas) of [Epirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Despotate_of_Epirus) or [Laskarids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laskaris) of [Nicaea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Nicaea).[^cite_note-54]&#x20;



A relief of the [Scuola degli Albanesi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuola_degli_Albanesi) commemorating the [siege of Shkodra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Shkodra). It illustrates [Sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Sultan) [Mehmet II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmet_II) laying siege to the Albanian town of Scutari, then part of the [Venetian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venetian_Empire).

Towards the end of the 12th and beginning of the 13th centuries, [Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbia_\(medieval\)) and [Venetians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venetian_Empire) began to take possession of the territory.[^cite_note-Barbarian_Invasions-55] The [ethnogenesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnogenesis) of the Albanians is uncertain; however, the first undisputed mention of Albanians dates back in historical records from 1079 or 1080 in a work by [Michael Attaleiates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Attaleiates), who referred to the [Albanoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanoi) as having taken part in a revolt against Constantinople.[^cite_note-56] At this point, the Albanians were fully Christianised.&#x20;

After the dissolution of Arbanon, [Charles I of Anjou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_I_of_Anjou) concluded an agreement with the Albanian rulers, promising to protect them and their ancient liberties. In 1272, he established the [Kingdom of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Albania_\(medieval\)) and conquered regions back from the [Despotate of Epirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Despotate_of_Epirus). The kingdom claimed all of central Albania territory from [Dyrrhachium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyrrhachium) along the Adriatic Sea coast down to [Butrint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint). A Catholic political structure was a basis for the papal plans of spreading Catholicism in the Balkans. This plan also found the support of [Helen of Anjou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Helen_of_Serbia). Around 30 Catholic churches and monasteries were built during her rule, mainly in northern Albania.[^cite_note-57] Internal power struggles within the Byzantine Empire in the 14th century enabled Serbs' most powerful medieval ruler, [Stefan Dušan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Du%C5%A1an), to establish a [short-lived empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Empire) that included all of Albania except Durrës.[^cite_note-Barbarian_Invasions-55]&#x20;



Albanian principalities, c. 1390, excluding the [Despotate of Arta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Despotate_of_Arta)

In 1367, Albanian rulers established the [Despotate of Arta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Despotate_of_Arta). During that time, several [Albanian principalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_principalities) were established, notably the [Principality of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Albania_\(medieval\)), the [Principality of Kastrioti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Kastrioti), the [Lordship of Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lordship_of_Berat), and the [Principality of Dukagjini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Dukagjini). In the first half of the 15th century, the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md) invaded most of Albania, and the [League of Lezhë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Lezh%C3%AB) was organized under [Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg) as a ruler, who became the national hero of the Albanian medieval history.[^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59]&#x20;

#### Ottoman Empire

Main article: [Albania under the Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania_under_the_Ottoman_Empire)

See also: [Albanian rebellion against the Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg#Rebellion_against_the_Ottomans)

Gjergj Kastrioti Skanderbeg

After serving the [Ottoman Empire](22278.md) for nearly 20 years, [Gjergj Kastrioti Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjergj_Kastrioti_Skanderbeg) deserted and began a successful [rebellion against the empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg%27s_rebellion) that halted Ottoman advance into Europe for 25 years.

Ali Pasha Tepelena

[Ali Pasha Tepelena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Pasha_Tepelena) was a powerful autonomous Ottoman-Albanian ruler, governing over the [Pashalik of Yanina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pashalik_of_Yanina).

With the [fall of Constantinople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_of_Constantinople), the Ottoman Empire entered an extended period of military conquest and expansion, with its borders gradually expanding [deep into Southeast Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_wars_in_Europe). In 1385, they reached the [Albanian Ionian Sea Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Ionian_Sea_Coast), while in 1415, they established their garrisons across [Southern Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Albania), eventually occupying most of Albania in 1431.[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-Hupchich110-61] Thousands of Albanians consequently fled to [Western Europe](33800.md), particularly to [Calabria](44772.md), [Naples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Naples), [Ragusa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Ragusa), and [Sicily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sicily), while numerous others sought protection at the often inaccessible [Mountains of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Albania).[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-Norris1993-53] Since the vast majority of Albanians were adherents of Christianity, they were considered an [inferior class of people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rayah), and they were discriminated against in various forms, including being subjected to heavy [taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jizya). Additionally, the [devshirme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devshirme) system allowed the [sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultan) to collect a requisite percentage of Christian adolescents from their families to compose the [Janissary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janissary).[^cite_note-63] The Ottoman conquest was also accompanied by the gradual process of [Islamisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamisation) and the rapid construction of mosques.&#x20;

A prosperous and longstanding revolution erupted after the formation of the [League of Lezhë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Lezh%C3%AB) until the [fall of Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Shkodra) under the leadership of [Gjergj Kastrioti Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg), who consistently defeated major Ottoman armies led by [Sultans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultan) [Murad II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murad_II) and [Mehmed II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmed_II). Skanderbeg managed to unite several of the Albanian principalities, amongst them the [Arianitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arianiti_family), [Dukagjinis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dukagjini_family), [Zaharias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaharia_family), and [Thopias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thopia_family), and establish a centralised authority over most of the non-conquered territories, becoming the lord of Albania.[^cite_note-League_of_Lezhë-64] The Ottoman Empire's expansion ground to a halt during the time that Skanderbeg's forces resisted, and he has been credited with being one of the main reasons for the delay of Ottoman expansion into Western Europe, giving the Italian principalities more time to better prepare for the [Ottoman arrival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_conquest_of_Otranto).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHodgkinson2005240-65][^cite_note-66] However, the failure of most European nations, with the exception of [Naples](55880.md), to adequately support him, along with the failure of [Pope Pius II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Pius_II)'s plans to organise a promised crusade against the Ottomans, signified that none of Skanderbeg's victories permanently hindered the Ottomans from invading the western Balkans.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHodgkinson2005xii-67][^cite_note-Pitcher1968-68]&#x20;

Despite his competence as a military leader, Skanderbeg's victories over the [Ottoman Empire's military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_the_Ottoman_Empire) had only a short-term impact, as his forces were unable to halt the Ottomans' advancement permanently; therefore, they successfully delayed the final conquest of Albanian lands only temporarily. The constant Ottoman invasions caused enormous destruction to Albania, greatly reducing the population and destroying flocks of livestock and crops. Besides surrender, there was no possible way Skanderbeg would be able to halt the Ottoman invasions despite his military forces achieving several consecutive victories against them. Among other factors, his manpower and resources were insufficient for a prolonged war, preventing him from expanding the war efforts and driving the Turks from the Albanian borders. Albania was therefore doomed to face an unending series of Ottoman attacks until it eventually fell years after his death.[^cite_note-69]&#x20;

When the Ottomans were gaining a firm foothold in the region, Albanian towns were organised into four principal [sanjaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjak). The government fostered trade by settling a sizeable Jewish colony of refugees fleeing persecution in Spain. [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB) saw passing through its ports imported merchandise from Europe, such as velvets, cotton goods, mohairs, carpets, spices, and leather from [Bursa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bursa) and [Constantinople](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantinople). Some citizens of Vlorë even had business associates throughout Europe.[^cite_note-Arnawutlu-70]&#x20;

The phenomenon of Islamisation among the Albanians became primarily widespread from the 17th century and continued into the 18th century.[^cite_note-referenceworks.brillonline-71] Islam offered them equal opportunities and advancement within the Ottoman Empire. However, according to some scholars, motives for conversion were diverse depending on the context; although the lack of source material significantly hampers efforts to investigate such issues.[^cite_note-referenceworks.brillonline-71] As a result of the Ottomans' increasing suppression of Catholicism, most Catholic Albanians converted in the 17th century, while Orthodox Albanians followed suit mainly in the following century.[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73]&#x20;

Since the Albanian-inhabited regions were considered strategically important, Albanians made up a significant proportion of the Ottoman Empire's military and bureaucracy. Consequently, many Muslim Albanians attained important political and military positions, while their work made a significant cultural contribution to the broader [Muslim world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_world).[^cite_note-referenceworks.brillonline-71] Enjoying this privileged position, they held various high administrative positions, which, among others, included over two dozen Albanians serving as [grand viziers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_vizier) of the Ottoman Empire. Additionally, other distinguished Albanians included members of the prominent [Köprülü family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6pr%C3%BCl%C3%BC_family), [Zagan Pasha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zagan_Pasha), [Muhammad Ali of Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali_of_Egypt), and [Ali Pasha of Tepelena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Pasha_of_Yanina). Furthermore, two Ottoman sultans, [Bayezid II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayezid_II) and [Mehmed III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehmed_III), were born to mothers of Albanian ethnic backgrounds.[^cite_note-Arnawutlu-70][^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75]&#x20;

### Rilindja



[Dora d'Istria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dora_d%27Istria) was among the main advocates in Europe for the [Albanian cause](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Renaissance).

[^cite_note-76]

The [Albanian Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_National_Awakening) was a period with its roots in the late 18th century and continuing into the 19th century, during which the Albanian people gathered spiritual and intellectual strength for an independent cultural and political life within an [independent nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Albania). Modern [Albanian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Albania) flourished too, especially [Albanian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_literature) and [arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_art), and was frequently linked to the influences of the [Romanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism) and [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment) principles.[^cite_note-77] Prior to the [rise of nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rise_of_nationalism_in_the_Ottoman_Empire), Ottoman authorities suppressed any expression of national unity or conscience by the Albanian people.&#x20;

The victory of Russia over the Ottoman Empire following the [Russian-Ottoman Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russo-Turkish_wars) resulted the execution of the [Treaty of San Stefano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_San_Stefano) which assigned Albanian-populated lands to their Slavic and Greek neighbours. However, the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland) and [Austro-Hungarian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria-Hungary) consequently blocked the arrangement and caused the [Treaty of Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Berlin_\(1878\)). From this point, Albanians started to organise themselves with the goal to protect and unite the Albanian-populated lands into a unitary nation, leading to the formation of the [League of Prizren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Prizren). The league had initially the assistance of the Ottoman authorities whose position was based on the religious solidarity of Muslim people and landlords connected with the [Ottoman administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Porte). They favoured and protected the Muslim solidarity and called for defence of Muslim lands simultaneously constituting the reason for titling the league [Committee of the Real Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Prizren#Formation).[^cite_note-78]&#x20;

Approximately 300 Muslims participated in the assembly composed by delegates from Bosnia, the [administrator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjak-bey) of the [Sanjak of Prizren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanjak_of_Prizren) as representatives of the central authorities and no delegates from [Vilayet of Scutari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scutari_vilayet).[^cite_note-79] Signed by only 47 Muslim deputies, the league issued the [Kararname](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kararname_\(League_of_Prizren\)) that contained a proclamation that the people from northern Albania, [Epirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epirus) and [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md) are willing to defend the [territorial integrity](31335.md) of the Ottoman Empire by all possible means against the troops of [Bulgaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Bulgaria), [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbia) and [Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Montenegro).[^cite_note-albanianhistory.net-80]&#x20;

Ottomans authorities cancelled their assistance when the league, under [Abdyl Frashëri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdyl_Frash%C3%ABri), became focused on working towards Albanian autonomy and requested merging four [vilayets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vilayet)—[Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_vilayet), [Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scutari_vilayet), [Monastir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manastir_vilayet) and [Ioannina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janina_vilayet)—into a unified vilayet, the [Albanian Vilayet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Vilayet). The league used military force to prevent the annexing areas of [Plav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plav_Municipality) and [Gusinje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gusinje) assigned to Montenegro. After several successful battles with Montenegrin troops, such as the [Battle of Novšiće](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Nov%C5%A1i%C4%87e), the league was forced to retreat from their contested regions. The league was later defeated by the Ottoman army sent by the sultan.[^cite_note-League_of_Prizren-81]&#x20;

#### Independence

Main article: [Independent Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Albania)

Further information: [Massacres of Albanians in the Balkan Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacres_of_Albanians_in_the_Balkan_Wars)



[Ismail Qemali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Qemali) is regarded as the [founding father](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_founders#Albania) of the modern Albanian nation.

Albania [declared independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Declaration_of_Independence) from the Ottoman Empire on 28 November 1912, accompanied by the establishment of the [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elderly_Assembly) and [Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_Government_of_Albania) by the [Assembly of Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assembly_of_Vlor%C3%AB) on 4 December.[^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83][^cite_note-84] Its sovereignty was recognised by the [Conference of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London_Conference_of_1912%E2%80%931913). On 29 July 1913, the [Treaty of London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_London_\(1913\)) delineated the borders of the country and its neighbors, leaving many Albanians outside Albania, predominantly [partitioned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_\(politics\)) between [Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Montenegro), [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbia), and [Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Greece).[^cite_note-85]&#x20;

Headquartered in Vlorë, the [International Commission of Control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Control_Commission_\(Albania\)) was established on 15 October 1913 to take care of the administration of Albania until its own political institutions were in order.[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87] The [International Gendarmerie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Gendarmerie) was established as the [Principality of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Albania)'s first law enforcement agency. In November, the first gendarmerie members arrived in the country. [Wilhelm of Wied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm,_Prince_of_Albania) *(Princ Vilhelm Vidi)* was selected as the first prince of the principality.[^cite_note-88] On 7 March, he arrived in the provisional capital of [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs) and began to organise his government, appointing [Turhan Pasha Përmeti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turhan_Pasha_P%C3%ABrmeti) to form the first Albanian cabinet. The pro-Ottoman forces had offered the throne to the Ottoman war minister of Albanian origin, [Ahmed Izzet Pasha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Izzet_Pasha).[^cite_note-Elsie-89] The pro-Ottoman peasants believed that the new regime was a tool of the six Christian Great Powers and local landowners, who owned half of the arable land.[^cite_note-90]&#x20;

In February 1914, the [Autonomous Republic of Northern Epirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_Republic_of_Northern_Epirus) was proclaimed in [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr) by the local [Greek population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeks_in_Albania) against incorporation to Albania. This initiative was short-lived, and in 1921 the southern provinces were incorporated into the Albanian Principality.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92] High-ranking Orthodox clergy were among the strongest opponents of the Albanian National Awakening. Reports from the period, including those from international observers, documented the burning of villages and civilian casualties. Nationalist Orthodox insurgents were accused of targeting Muslim Albanian populations to consolidate territorial claims for Greece.[^cite_note-italianattitudetowards-93] Meanwhile, the [revolt of Albanian peasants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1914%E2%80%931915_Muslim_revolts_in_Albania) against the regime erupted under the leadership of the group of Muslim clerics gathered around [Essad Pasha Toptani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essad_Pasha_Toptani), who proclaimed himself the savior of Albania and Islam.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95] To gain the support of the [Mirdita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mirdita_\(tribe\)) Catholic volunteers from northern Albania, Wilhelm appointed their leader [Prênk Bibë Doda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pr%C3%AAnk_Bib%C3%AB_Doda) as foreign minister of the Principality of Albania. In May and June 1914, the International Gendarmerie was joined by [Isa Boletini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isa_Boletini) and his men, mostly from [Kosovo](17391.md),[^cite_note-Elsie-89] and the rebels defeated northern Mirdita Catholics, capturing most of central Albania by the end of August.[^cite_note-Elsie-89] Wilhelm's regime collapsed, and he left the country on 3 September.[^cite_note-96]&#x20;

### First Republic

Main article: [First Republic of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Republic_\(1925%E2%80%931928\))

See also: [World War I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_I_in_Albania) and [World War II in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II_in_Albania)



[Fan Noli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fan_Noli) played a significant role in the 20th century, advocating for Albanian independence and cultural revival, while also serving as [Prime Minister](259136.md) in 1924 and later as the head of the [Albanian Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Orthodox_Church).

The [interwar period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period) was marked by persistent economic and social difficulties, political instability and foreign interventions.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97][^cite_note-Vickers-98] After World War I, Albania lacked an established government and internationally recognised borders, rendering it vulnerable to neighboring entities such as Greece, Italy, and Yugoslavia, all of which sought to expand their influence.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] This led to political uncertainty, highlighted in 1918 when the [Congress of Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_Durr%C3%ABs) sought [Paris Peace Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Peace_Conference_\(1919%E2%80%931920\)) protection but was denied, further complicating Albania's position on the international stage. Territorial tensions escalated as Yugoslavia and particularly Serbia sought control of northern Albania, while Greece aimed dominance in southern Albania. The situation deteriorated in 1919 when the Serbs launched attacks on Albanian inhabitants, among others in [Gusinje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gusinje) and [Plav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plav,_Montenegro), resulting in [massacres and large-scale displacement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plav%E2%80%93Gusinje_massacres_\(1912%E2%80%931913\)).[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97][^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100] Meanwhile, Italian influence continued to expand during this time, driven by economic interests and political ambitions.[^cite_note-Vickers-98][^cite_note-Gerwarth-101]&#x20;

[Fan Noli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fan_Noli), renowned for his idealism, became prime minister in 1924, with a vision to institute a Western-style constitutional government, abolish feudalism, counter Italian influence, and enhance critical sectors, including infrastructure, education and healthcare.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] He faced resistance from former allies, who had assisted in the removal of [Zog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zog_I) from power, and struggled to secure foreign aid to implement his agenda. Noli's decision to establish diplomatic ties with the Soviet Union, an adversary of the Serbian elite, ignited allegations of [bolshevism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolshevism) from Belgrade.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] This in turn led to increased pressure from Italy and culminated in Zog's restoration to authority. In 1928, Zog transitioned Albania from a republic to a monarchy that garnered backing from [fascist Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist_Italy), with Zog assuming the title of King Zog I. Key constitutional changes dissolved the Senate and established a unicameral National Assembly while preserving Zog's authoritative powers.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97]&#x20;

In 1939, Italy under [Benito Mussolini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Mussolini) launched a military invasion of Albania, resulting in the exile of Zog and the creation of an [Italian protectorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Albania_in_personal_union_with_Italy).[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-isbn0-8240-7029-1-103] As World War II progressed, Italy aimed to expand its territorial dominion in the Balkans, including territorial claims on regions of Greece ([Chameria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chameria)), Macedonia, Montenegro and Kosovo. These ambitions laid the foundation of Greater Albania, which aimed to unite all areas with Albanian-majority populations into a single country.[^cite_note-BogdaniLoughlin2007-104] In 1943, as Italy's control declined, [Nazi Germany](21212.md) assumed control of Albania, subjecting Albanians to forced labour, economic exploitation and repression under [German rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Albania_\(1943-1944\)). This period saw the forced expulsion and massacres of the [Cham Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cham_Albanians), a predominantly Muslim group. The religious divide was used as a justification for violence, with some Orthodox locals reportedly identifying Muslim neighbors to paramilitary groups, leading to massacres and the forced expulsion of thousands.[^cite_note-italianattitudetowards-93][^cite_note-Morrock2010-105] The tide shifted in 1944 when Albanian partisan forces, under the leadership of [Enver Hoxha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enver_Hoxha) and other communist leaders, successfully liberated Albania from German occupation.[^cite_note-Albanian_Nationalism-106]&#x20;

### Socialist Republic

Further information: [Expulsion of Cham Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expulsion_of_Cham_Albanians)



[Enver Hoxha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enver_Hoxha) was the founding leader of communist Albania and its ruler for over four decades, implementing a regime marked by [authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism) and [isolationism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolationism).

The establishment of the [People's Republic of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Socialist_Republic_of_Albania) under the leadership of Hoxha was a significant epoch in Albanian history.[^cite_note-107] Hoxha's regime embraced [Marxist–Leninist ideologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism%E2%80%93Leninism) and implemented [authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Authoritarianism) policies, including prohibition of religious practices, severe restrictions on travel, and abolition of private property rights.[^cite_note-HRW_C-108] It was also defined by a persistent pattern of purges, extensive repression, instances of betrayal, and hostility to external influences.[^cite_note-HRW_C-108] Any form of opposition or resistance to his rule was met with expeditious and severe consequences, such as internal exile, extended imprisonment, and execution.[^cite_note-HRW_C-108]&#x20;

The regime confronted a multitude of challenges, including widespread poverty, illiteracy, health crises and gender inequality.[^cite_note-Albanian_Nationalism-106] In response, Hoxha initiated a modernisation initiative aimed at attaining economic and social liberation and transforming Albania into an industrial society.[^cite_note-Albanian_Nationalism-106] The regime placed a high priority on the diversification of the economy through Soviet-style industrialisation, comprehensive infrastructure development such as the introduction of a transformative [railway system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hekurudha_Shqiptare), expansion of education and healthcare services, elimination of adult illiteracy, and targeted advancements in areas such as women's rights.[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112]&#x20;



[Bunkers in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bunkers_in_Albania) were constructed to prevent potential external invasions. By 1983, approximately 173,371 bunkers were scattered throughout its territory.

[^cite_note-113]

Diplomacy under Hoxha was characterised by notable conflicts.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] Initially aligned with Yugoslavia as a satellite state, the relationship deteriorated as Yugoslavia aimed to incorporate Albania within its territory.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] Subsequently, Albania established relations with the Soviet Union and engaged trade agreements with other Eastern European countries but experienced disagreements over Soviet policies, leading to strained ties with Moscow and diplomatic separation in 1961.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] Simultaneously, tensions with the West heightened with Albania's refusal to hold free elections and allegations of Western support for [anti-communist uprisings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-communism). Albania's enduring partnership was with China; it sided with Beijing during the [Sino-Soviet conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Soviet_split), resulting in severed ties with the Soviet Union and withdrawal from the [Warsaw Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Pact) in response to the invasion of Czechoslovakia in 1968. But their relations stagnated in 1970, prompting both to reassess their commitment, and Albania actively reduced its dependence on China.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97]&#x20;

Under Hoxha's regime, Albania underwent a widespread campaign targeting religious clergy of various faiths, resulting in public persecution and executions, particularly targeting Muslims, Roman Catholics and Eastern Orthodox adherents.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] In 1946, religious estates underwent nationalisation, coinciding with the closure or transformation of religious institutions into various other purposes.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97] This culminated in 1976, when Albania became the world's first constitutionally atheist state.[^cite_note-Elsie_p27-114] Under this regime, citizens were forced to renounce their religious beliefs, adopt a secular way of life, and embrace socialist ideology.[^cite_note-Zickel/Iwaskiw-97][^cite_note-Elsie_p27-114]&#x20;

### Fourth Republic



In 1988, the first foreigners were allowed to walk into the car-free [Skanderbeg Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg_Square) in [Tirana](31075.md).

After four decades of communism paired with the [revolutions of 1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutions_of_1989), Albania witnessed a notable rise in political activism, particularly among students, which led to a transformation in the prevailing order. After the first [multi-party elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-party_elections) of [1991](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1991_Albanian_parliamentary_election), the communist party maintained a stronghold in the parliament until its defeat in the [parliamentary elections of 1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Albanian_parliamentary_election) directed by the [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Albania).[^cite_note-115]&#x20;

Considerable economic and financial resources were devoted to [pyramid schemes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyramid_schemes_in_Albania) that were widely supported by the government. The schemes swept up somewhere between one sixth and one third of the population.[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117] Despite the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md)'s warnings, [Sali Berisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sali_Berisha) defended the schemes as large investment firms, leading more people to redirect their remittances and sell their homes and cattle for cash to deposit in the schemes.[^cite_note-118] The schemes began to collapse in late 1996, leading many of the investors to join initially peaceful protests against the government, requesting their money back. The protests [turned violent in February 1997](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Albanian_civil_unrest) as government forces responded by firing on the demonstrators. In March, the Police and Republican Guard deserted, leaving their armories open. These were promptly emptied by militias and criminal gangs. The resulting civil war caused a wave of evacuations of foreign nationals and refugees.[^cite_note-119]&#x20;

The crisis led both [Aleksandër Meksi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksand%C3%ABr_Meksi) and [Sali Berisha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sali_Berisha) to resign from office in the wake of the general election. In April 1997, [Operation Alba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Alba), a U.N. peacekeeping force led by Italy, entered Albania with two goals: to assist with the evacuation of expatriates and secure the ground for international organisations. The main international organisation involved was the [Western European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_European_Union)'s multinational [Albanian Police](6225413.md) element, which worked with the government to restructure the judicial system and simultaneously the Albanian police.&#x20;

### Contemporary

See also: [Accession of Albania to the EU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Albania_to_the_European_Union) and [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania%E2%80%93NATO_relations)

Further information: [2019 Albania earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Albania_earthquake) and [COVID-19 pandemic in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Albania)



The [earthquake of November 2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Albania_earthquake) was the strongest to hit Albania in more than four decades.

[^cite_note-120]

After [its communist system disintegrated](17545990.md), Albania embarked on an active path towards [Westernisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westernization) with the ambition to obtain membership in the [European Union](9317.md) (EU) and the North Atlantic Treaty Organisation ([NATO](21133.md)).[^cite_note-121] A notable milestone was reached in 2009 when the country attained [attained membership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albania%E2%80%93NATO_relations) in NATO, marking a pioneering achievement among the nations of southeastern Europe.[^cite_note-122][^cite_note-123] In adherence to its vision for further integration into the EU, it [formally applied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Albania_to_the_European_Union) for membership on 28 April 2009.[^cite_note-124] Another milestone was reached on 24 June 2014, when the country was granted [official candidate status](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Potential_enlargement_of_the_European_Union).[^cite_note-EU_CS-125]&#x20;

[Edi Rama](277805.md) of the [Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_of_Albania) won both the [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Albanian_parliamentary_election) and [2017 parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Albanian_parliamentary_election). As [prime minister](259136.md), he implemented numerous reforms focused on modernising the economy, as well as democratising state institutions, including the judiciary and law enforcement. Unemployment has steadily declined.[^cite_note-126] Rama placed [gender equality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_equality) at the centre of his agenda; since 2017 almost 50% of the ministers have been female, the largest number of women serving in the country's history.[^cite_note-127] During the [2021 parliamentary elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Albanian_parliamentary_election), the ruling Socialist Party led by Rama secured its third consecutive victory, winning nearly half of votes and enough seats in parliament to govern alone.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129]&#x20;

On 26 November 2019, a 6.4 magnitude [earthquake ravaged Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Albania_earthquake), with the [epicentre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epicenter) about 16 km (10 mi) southwest of [Mamurras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mamurras).[^cite_note-130] The tremor was felt in Tirana and in places as far away as [Taranto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taranto), Italy, and [Belgrade](55904.md), Serbia, while the most affected areas were Durrës and [Kodër-Thumanë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kod%C3%ABr-Thuman%C3%AB).[^cite_note-131] Comprehensive response to the earthquake included substantial humanitarian aid from the [Albanian diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_diaspora) and various countries around the world.[^cite_note-132]&#x20;

On 9 March 2020, [COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Albania) was confirmed to have spread to Albania.[^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134] From March to June 2020, the government declared a [state of emergency](298608.md) as a measure to limit the virus's spread.[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136][^cite_note-137] The country's COVID-19 [vaccination campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_vaccination_in_Albania) started on 11 January 2021, but as of 11 August 2021, the total number of vaccines administered in Albania was 1,280,239 doses.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139]&#x20;

\
&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Albania)



The [Albanian Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Alps) are an extension and simultaneously the highest section of the [Dinaric Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinaric_Alps).

Albania lies along the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md) on the [Balkan Peninsula](4829.md) in [South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Europe) and [Southeast Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeastern_Europe), and has an area of 28,748 km<sup>2</sup> (11,100 sq mi).[^cite_note-Albania_pdf-140] It is bordered by the [Adriatic Sea](23275478.md) to the west, [Montenegro](20760.md) to the northwest, [Kosovo](17391.md) to the northeast, [North Macedonia](23564616.md) to the east, [Greece](12108.md) to the south, and the [Ionian Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionian_Sea) to the southwest. It is between latitudes [42°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/42nd_parallel_north) and [39° N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/39th_parallel_north) and longitudes [21°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_meridian_east) and [19° E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_meridian_east). Geographic coordinates include [Vërmosh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%ABrmosh) at 42° 35' 34" northern latitude as the northernmost point, [Konispol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konispol) at 39° 40' 0" northern latitude as the southernmost, [Sazan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sazan_Island) at 19° 16' 50" eastern longitude as the westernmost, and [Vërnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/V%C3%ABrnik) at 21° 1' 26" eastern longitude as the easternmost.[^cite_note-141] [Mount Korab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Korab), rising at 2,764 m (9,068.24 ft) [above the Adriatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metres_above_the_Adriatic), is the highest point, while the Mediterranean Sea, at 0 m (0.00 ft), is the lowest. The country extends 148 km (92 mi) from east to west and around 340 km (211 mi) from north to south.&#x20;

Albania has a diverse and varied landscape with mountains and hills that traverse its territory in various directions. There are extensive mountain ranges, including the [Albanian Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Alps) in the north, the [Korab Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korab) in the east, the [Pindus Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindus_Mountains) in the southeast, the [Ceraunian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceraunian_Mountains) in the southwest, and the [Skanderbeg Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg_Mountains) in the centre. In the northwest is [Lake Shkodër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Skadar), Southern Europe's largest lake.[^cite_note-142] Towards the southeast is [Lake Ohrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ohrid), one of the world's oldest continuously existing lakes.[^cite_note-143] Farther south, the expanse includes the [Large](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Prespa) and [Small Lake of Prespa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_Prespa_Lake), some of the Balkans' highest lakes. Rivers rise mostly in the east and discharge into the Adriatic and Ionian Seas. The longest river is the [Drin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drin_\(river\)), which starts at the confluence of its two headwaters, the [Black Drin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Drin) and [White Drin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Drin). Of particular concern is the [Vjosë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vjosa), one of Europe's last intact large river systems.&#x20;

[Forest cover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forest_cover) is around 29.% of the total land area, equivalent to 788,900 hectares (ha) of forest in 2020, up from 788,800 hectares (ha) in 1990. Of the naturally regenerating forest 11% was reported to be [primary forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_forest) (consisting of native tree species with no clearly visible indications of human activity) and around 0% of the forest area was found within protected areas. In 2015, 97% of the forest area was reported to be under [public ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_ownership), 3% [private ownership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_property) and 0% with ownership listed as other or unknown.[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145]&#x20;

### Climate



[Gjipe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjipe) is located on the confluence of the [Adriatic](23275478.md) and [Ionian Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ionian_Sea), on the [Albanian Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Riviera).

The [climate of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Albania) exhibits a distinguished level of variability and diversity because of the differences in latitude, longitude and altitude.[^cite_note-climate-146][^cite_note-climate2-147][^cite_note-climate1-148] There are five primary climatic types, spanning from [Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate) and [subtropical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate) in the western half to [oceanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_climate), [continental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_continental_climate) and [subarctic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subarctic_climate) in the eastern half of the country.[^cite_note-149] The coastal regions are the warmest areas, while the northern and eastern regions encompassing the Albanian Alps and the Korab Mountains the coldest areas in the country.[^cite_note-climate3-150] Throughout the year, the average monthly temperatures fluctuate, ranging from −1 [°C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celsius) (30 [°F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahrenheit)) during the winter months to 21.8 °C (71.2 °F) in the summer months. The highest recorded temperature of 43.9 °C (111.0 °F) was observed in [Kuçovë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ku%C3%A7ov%C3%AB) on 18 July 1973, while the lowest temperature of −29 °C (−20 °F) was recorded in Shtyllë, [Librazhd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Librazhd) on 9 January 2017.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152]&#x20;

Albania receives most of the precipitation in winter months and less in summer months.[^cite_note-climate2-147] The average precipitation is about 1,485 millimetres (58.5 inches).[^cite_note-climate3-150] The mean annual precipitation ranges between 600 and 3,000 millimetres (24 and 118 inches) depending on geographical location.[^cite_note-climate1-148] The northwestern and [southeastern highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Mountain_Region_\(Albania\)) receive the intenser amount of precipitation, whilst the northeastern and southwestern highlands as well as the [Western Lowlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Coastal_Lowlands) the more limited amount.[^cite_note-climate3-150] The Albanian Alps are among the most humid regions of Europe, receiving at least 3,100 mm (122.0 in) of rain annually.[^cite_note-climate3-150] Four [glaciers](12463.md) within these mountains were discovered at a relatively low altitude of 2,000 metres (6,600 ft), which is rare for such a southerly latitude.[^cite_note-153]&#x20;

### Biodiversity

Main article: [Biodiversity of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_of_Albania)

See also: [Ecoregions in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ecoregions_in_Albania)



The [golden eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_eagle) is the national symbol and animal of Albania.

A [biodiversity hotspot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot), Albania possesses an exceptionally rich and contrasting biodiversity on account of its geographical location at the centre of the Mediterranean Sea and the great diversity in its climatic, geological and hydrological conditions.[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-155] Because of remoteness, the mountains and hills are covered with forests, trees and grasses that are essential to the lives for a wide variety of animals, including the endangered [Balkan lynx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balkan_lynx) and [brown bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brown_bear), as well as the [wildcat](269357.md), [grey wolf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_wolf), [red fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_fox), [golden jackal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_jackal), [Egyptian vulture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egyptian_vulture) and [golden eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_eagle).[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159]&#x20;

The estuaries, wetlands and lakes are extraordinarily important for the [greater flamingo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_flamingo), [pygmy cormorant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pygmy_cormorant) and the extremely rare and perhaps the most iconic bird of the country, the [dalmatian pelican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalmatian_pelican).[^cite_note-160] Of particular importance are the [Mediterranean monk seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_monk_seal), [loggerhead sea turtle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loggerhead_sea_turtle) and [green sea turtle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_sea_turtle) that use to nest on the country's coastal waters and shores.&#x20;

In terms of [phytogeography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phytogeography), Albania is part of the [Boreal Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boreal_Kingdom) and stretches specifically within the [Illyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyria) province of the [Circumboreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circumboreal_Region) and [Mediterranean Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Basin). Its territory can be subdivided into four terrestrial ecoregions of the [Palearctic realm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palearctic_realm) namely within the [Illyrian deciduous forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrian_deciduous_forests), [Balkan mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balkan_mixed_forests), [Pindus Mountains mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindus_Mountains_mixed_forests) and [Dinaric Mountains mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinaric_Mountains_mixed_forests).[^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162] Approximately 3,500 different species of plants can be found in Albania which refers principally to a Mediterranean and Eurasian character. The country maintains a vibrant tradition of herbal and medicinal practices. At the minimum 300 plants growing locally are used in the preparation of herbs and medicines.[^cite_note-163] The trees within the forests are primarily [fir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fir), [oak](39696.md), [beech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beech) and [pine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pine).&#x20;

### Conservation



The [islets of Ksamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islets_of_Ksamil) lie in the [Butrint National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint_National_Park).

Albania has been an active participant in numerous international agreements and conventions aimed at strengthing its commitment to the preservation and sustainable management of biological diversity. Since 1994, the country is a party to the [Convention on Biological Diversity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_on_Biological_Diversity) and its associated [Cartagena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartagena_Protocol_on_Biosafety) and [Nagoya Protocols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagoya_Protocol).[^cite_note-CBD-164] To uphold these commitments, it has developed and implemented a comprehensive [National Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biodiversity_action_plan).[^cite_note-CBD-164] Albania has established a partnership with the [International Union for Conservation of Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Union_for_Conservation_of_Nature), advancing its conservation efforts on both national and international scales.&#x20;

As a signatory to the [Ramsar Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramsar_Convention), Albania has granted special recognition upon four wetlands, designating them as Wetlands of International Importance, including [Buna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buna_\(Adriatic_Sea\))-Shkodër, [Butrint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint_Lagoon), [Karavasta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karavasta_Lagoon) and Prespa.[^cite_note-165] The dedication to protection extends further into the sphere of [UNESCO](21786641.md)'s [World Network of Biosphere Reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Network_of_Biosphere_Reserves), operating within the framework of the [Man and the Biosphere Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_and_the_Biosphere_Programme), evidenced by its engagement in the [Ohrid-Prespa Transboundary Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohrid-Prespa_Transboundary_Biosphere_Reserve).[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167] Albania is host to two natural [World Heritage Sites](44940.md), which encompass the Ohrid region and both the [Gashi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gashi_\(river\)) and [Rrajca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rrajc%C3%AB) as part of [Ancient and Primeval Beech Forests of the Carpathians and Other Regions of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_and_Primeval_Beech_Forests_of_the_Carpathians_and_Other_Regions_of_Europe).[^cite_note-Ancient_Primeval_Beech_Forests-168]&#x20;

#### Protected areas



The [lagoon of Karavasta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lagoon_of_Karavasta) within the [Divjakë-Karavasta National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divjak%C3%AB-Karavasta_National_Park)

The [protected areas of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Albania) are designated and managed by the government.[^cite_note-New_NP-169] There are 12 [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_park), 4 [ramsar sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramsar_site), 1 [biosphere reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biosphere_reserve) and 786 other types of conservation reserves.[^cite_note-New_NP-169][^cite_note-170] The [Alps of Albania National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alps_of_Albania_National_Park) is surrounded amidst the towering peaks of the Albanian Alps. In the east, portions of the rugged Korab, [Nemërçka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nem%C3%ABr%C3%A7ka) and Shebenik Mountains are conserved within the boundaries of [Fir of Hotovë-Dangëlli National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fir_of_Hotov%C3%AB-Dang%C3%ABlli_National_Park), [Shebenik National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebenik_National_Park) and [Prespa National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prespa_National_Park_\(Albania\)).&#x20;

To the south, the [Ceraunian Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceraunian_Mountains) define the Albanian Ionian Sea Coast, shaping the landscape of [Llogara National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Llogara_National_Park), which extends into the [Karaburun Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaburun_Peninsula,_Albania), forming the [Karaburun-Sazan Marine Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaburun-Sazan_Marine_Park). Further southward lies [Butrint National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint_National_Park), occupying a peninsula surrounded by the Lake of Butrint and the [Vivari Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vivari_Channel). In the west, stretching along the Albanian Adriatic Sea Coast, the [Divjakë-Karavasta National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divjak%C3%AB-Karavasta_National_Park) boasts the extensive [Karavasta Lagoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karavasta_Lagoon), one of the largest lagoon systems in the Mediterranean Sea. Europe's first wild river national park, [Vjosa National Park](73303962.md), safeguards the Vjosa River and its primary tributaries, which originates in the [Pindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pindus) Mountains and flows to the Adriatic Sea. [Dajti Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dajti_Mountain_National_Park), [Lurë-Dejë Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lur%C3%AB-Dej%C3%AB_Mountain_National_Park) and [Tomorr Mountain National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomorr_Mountain_National_Park) protect the mountainous terrain of the centre of Albania, including the [Tomorr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomorr) and Skanderbeg Mountains.&#x20;

#### Environmental issues



Temperature change in Albania. Each bar represents the temperature over a year.

[Environmental issues in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_Albania) notably encompass air and water pollution, climate change impacts, waste management shortcomings, biodiversity loss and imperative for nature conservation.[^cite_note-171][^cite_note-172] Climate change is predicted to exert significant impacts on the quality of life.[^cite_note-173] Albania is one of the European countries most at risk and vulnerable to natural disasters,[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-imfcountryreport-175][^cite_note-176] Rising sea levels are anticipated to negatively impact coastal communities and the tourism industry.[^cite_note-imfcountryreport-175]&#x20;

In 2023 Albania emitted 7.67 million tonnes of [greenhouse gases](21350772.md),[^cite_note-177] equivalent to 2.73 tonnes per person,[^cite_note-178] making it a relatively low emitting country. Albania has pledged a 20.9% reduction in greenhouse gas emissions by 2030 and [net zero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net-zero_emissions) by 2050.[^cite_note-179] The country has a moderate and improving performance in the [Environmental Performance Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_Performance_Index) with an overall ranking of 62 out of 180 countries in 2022.[^cite_note-180] However, its ranking has decreased since its highest placement at position 15 in the Environmental Performance Index of 2012.[^cite_note-181]&#x20;



The [Prespa National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prespa_National_Park_\(Albania\)) in southeastern Albania is part of the [European Green Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Green_Belt) and [Ohrid-Prespa Biosphere Reserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohrid-Prespa_Transboundary_Biosphere_Reserve).

## Government and politics

Main article: [Politics of Albania](67576.md)

See also: [Elections in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Albania) and [Government of Albania](67576.md)



[Bajram Begaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bajram_Begaj)\
[President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Albania)



[Edi Rama](277805.md)\
[Prime Minister](259136.md)

Since declaring independence in 1912, Albania has experienced a significant political transformation, traversing through distinct periods that included a monarchical rule, a communist regime and the eventual establishment of a democratic order.[^cite_note-182] In 1998, Albania transitioned into a [sovereign](23604120.md) [parliamentary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_republic) [constitutional republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_republic), marking a fundamental milestone in its political evolution.[^cite_note-Kushtetuta-183] Its governance structure operates under a [constitution](7878011.md) that serves as the principal document of the country.[^cite_note-184] The constitution is grounded in the principle of the [separation of powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers), with three arms of government that encompass the legislative embodied in the [Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Albania), the executive led by the [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Albania) as the ceremonial [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state) and the [prime minister](259136.md) as the functional [head of government](37570.md), and the judiciary with a hierarchy of courts, including the [constitutional](29207004.md) and [supreme courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Albania) as well as multiple [appeal](49668987.md) and [administrative courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_Courts_in_Albania).[^cite_note-Kushtetuta-183]&#x20;

The legal system is structured to protect its people's political rights, regardless of their ethnic, linguistic, racial, or religious affiliations.[^cite_note-Kushtetuta-183][^cite_note-FH2023-185] Despite these principles, there are significant human rights concerns that demand attention.[^cite_note-USDS_Politics-186] These concerns include issues related to the independence of the judiciary, the absence of a free media sector and the enduring problem of corruption within various governmental bodies, law enforcement agencies and other institutions.[^cite_note-USDS_Politics-186] As Albania pursues its path towards EU membership, active efforts are being made to achieve substantial improvements in these areas to align with EU criteria and standards.[^cite_note-FH2023-185]&#x20;

### Administrative divisions
