The [counties of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counties_of_Albania) are the first-level administrative units in Albania.

Albania is informally divided into three regions, the [Northern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Albania), [Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Albania) and [Southern Regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Albania). Since its Declaration of Independence in 1912, Albania has reformed its internal organisation 21 times. Presently, the primary [administrative units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Albania) are the 12 constituent [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counties_of_Albania) (*[qarqe](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/qarqe#Albanian)/[qarqet](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/qarqet#Albanian)*), which hold equal status under the law.[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-188][^cite_note-terref-189]&#x20;

The counties are made up of 61 second-level divisions known as [municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Albania) (*[bashki](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/bashki#Albanian)/[bashkia](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/bashkia#Albanian)*).[^cite_note-190] The municipalities are the first level of local governance, responsible for local needs and [law enforcement](11802631.md).[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-\:0-192][^cite_note-193] They unified and simplified the previous system of [urban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_municipalities_of_Albania) and [rural municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rural_municipalities_of_Albania) or [communes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communes_of_Albania) (*[komuna](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/komuna#Albanian)/[komunat](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/komunat#Albanian)*) in 2015.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-\:0-192] For smaller issues of local government, the municipalities are organised into 373 administrative units (*njësia*/*njësitë administrative*). There are 2,980 [villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villages_of_Albania) (*[fshatra](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/fshatra#Albanian)/[fshatrat](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/fshatrat#Albanian)*), neighborhoods or wards (*[lagje](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/lagje#Albanian)/[lagjet](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/lagjet#Albanian)*), and localities (*[lokalitete](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/lokalitete#Albanian)/[lokalitetet](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/lokalitetet#Albanian)*) previously used as administrative units.&#x20;

### Foreign relations



Assisted by the governments of [Kosovo](17391.md) and Albania, an official application for the inclusion of the [Arbëreshë people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arb%C3%ABresh%C3%AB_people) in the list of [UNESCO](21786641.md)'s [Intangible Cultural Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists) is being prepared.

[^cite_note-197]

Emerging from decades of isolation following the fall of communism, Albania has adopted a [foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Albania) orientation centred on active cooperation and engagement in international affairs. At the core of Albania's foreign policies lie a set of objectives, which encompass the commitment to protect its sovereignty and territorial integrity, the cultivation of diplomatic ties with other countries, advocating for [international recognition of Kosovo](15808914.md), addressing the concerns related to the [expulsion of Cham Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expulsion_of_Cham_Albanians), pursuing Euro-Atlantic integration and protecting the rights of the Albanians in [Kosovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians_in_Kosovo), [Greece](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians_in_greece), [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arb%C3%ABresh%C3%AB_people), [Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians_in_Montenegro), [North Macedonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians_in_Macedonia), [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians_in_south_Serbia) and the [diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_diaspora).[^cite_note-198]&#x20;

The external affairs underscore the country's dedication to regional stability and integration into major international institutions.[^cite_note-199] Albania became a member of the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN) in 1955, shortly after emerging from a period of isolation during the communist era.[^cite_note-200] Since obtaining candidate status in 2014, the country has embarked on a comprehensive reform agenda to align itself with European Union (EU) accession standards.[^cite_note-EU_CS-125]&#x20;

Albania and Kosovo maintain a fraternal relationship strengthened by their substantial cultural, ethnical and historical ties.[^cite_note-AlbKosRelation-201] Both countries foster enduring diplomatic ties, with Albania actively supporting Kosovo's development and international integration efforts.[^cite_note-AlbKosRelation-201] Its fundamental contribution to [Kosovo's path to independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Kosovo_declaration_of_independence) is underscored by its early [recognition of Kosovo's sovereignty](15808914.md) in 2008.[^cite_note-202] Furthermore, both governments hold annual joint meetings, displayed by the inaugural meeting in 2014, which serves as an official platform to enhance bilateral cooperation and reinforce their joint commitment to policies that promote the stability and prosperity of the broader Albanian region.[^cite_note-AlbKosRelation-201]&#x20;

### Military



[Albanian soldiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Land_Force) in the [Province of Kandahar](457241.md), Afghanistan

The [Albanian Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Armed_Forces) consist of [Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Land_Forces), [Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Air_Force) and [Naval Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Naval_Force) and constitute the military and paramilitary forces. They are led by a [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) under the supervision of the [Ministry of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_\(Albania\)) and by the president as the supreme commander during wartime. In times of peace its powers are executed through the prime minister and the defence minister.[^cite_note-constitution-203]&#x20;

The chief purpose of the armed forces is the defence of the independence, the [sovereignty](28791.md) and the [territorial integrity](31335.md) of the country, as well as the participation in humanitarian, combat, non-combat and peace support operations.[^cite_note-constitution-203] [Military service](938728.md) is voluntary since 2010 with the age of 19 being the legal minimum age for the duty.[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205]&#x20;

Albania has committed to increase the participations in multinational operations.[^cite_note-206] Since the fall of communism, the country has participated in six international missions but only one United Nations mission in [Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNOMIG), where it sent three military observers. Since February 2008, Albania has participated officially in NATO's [Operation Active Endeavor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Active_Endeavor) in the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md).[^cite_note-207] Albania reduced the number of active troops from 65,000 in 1988 to 14,500 in 2009.[^cite_note-208][^cite_note-209] The military consists mainly of a small fleet of aircraft and sea vessels. Increasing the military budget was one of the most important conditions for NATO integration. As of 1996 military spending was an estimated 1.5% of the country's GDP, only to peak in 2009 at 2% and fall again to 1.5%.[^cite_note-210]&#x20;

### Human rights



2016 LGBT+ Pride in [Tirana](31075.md)

Nearly 60% of women in rural areas suffer physical or psychological violence and nearly 8% are victims of sexual violence. Protection orders are often violated. In 2014 the Albanian Helsinki Committee reported that the number of female murder victims is high.[^cite_note-civilRightsDefenders-211] The Commissioner for Protection from Discrimination has raised concerns regarding the family registration law that discriminates against women. As a result, heads of households, who are overwhelmingly men, have the right to change family residency without their partners' permission.[^cite_note-civilRightsDefenders-211]&#x20;

In 2015, the association [ILGA-Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ILGA-Europe) ranked Albania 19th in terms of LGBT rights out of 49 observed European countries.[^cite_note-212] On the latest report in 2022, lack of progress caused Albania to be ranked the 28th country in Europe, among 49 countries observed.[^cite_note-213]&#x20;

## Economy



The metropolitan area of Durana, which encompasses the cities of [Tirana](31075.md) and [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs), functions as the principal economic hub of Albania.

[^cite_note-harvnb_unece_country-214]

Transitioning from a socialist [planned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planned_economy) to a [capitalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitalism) [mixed economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed_economy), Albania is classified as a [developing](78449.md) [open market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_market) with an [upper-middle income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GNI_\(nominal,_Atlas_method\)_per_capita#Upper-middle-income_group) status.[^cite_note-215][^cite_note-216] The [Albanian economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Albania) is characterised by a substantial [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy), comprising 48.6% of GDP, with key contributions from wholesale and retail trade, [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Albania), real estate, and logistics.[^cite_note-Eco:_Sectors-217] Following this, the industrial sector accounts for 20.2%, particularly influenced by construction, while the agricultural sector, including forestry, and fisheries, represents 18.5%, and the manufacturing sector contributes a modest 6.3% to the economy.[^cite_note-Eco:_Sectors-217] As of 2025, nominal gross domestic product (GDP) is \$26.911 billion with a GDP per capita of \$9,474.[^cite_note-218] The projected annual percentage change in economic growth for 2025 is 3.8%, with a forecasted moderation to 3.5% by 2029.[^cite_note-219]&#x20;

Albania has developed a comprehensive framework for international trade through several pivotal agreements with various regions. The [Stabilisation and Association Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stabilisation_and_Association_Process) with the EU, signed in 2006, is one of the most significant.[^cite_note-220] This agreement provides critical trade benefits and serves as a foundation for integration into the EU. In 2009, Albania signed a free trade agreement with the [European Free Trade Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Free_Trade_Association), which further expands trade opportunities and market access.[^cite_note-221] The country is a signatory to the [Central European Free Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_European_Free_Trade_Agreement) and [Organization of the Black Sea Economic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_the_Black_Sea_Economic_Cooperation), promoting regional trade and collaboration with neighboring countries.[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223] Bilateral trade relations have increased with Turkey, the United Kingdom and the United States.[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225]&#x20;

The 2023 [Economic Freedom of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_Freedom_of_the_World) report placed Albania in the 31st position, noting a considerable degree of economic independence and the effective adoption of market-oriented policies.[^cite_note-Economic_Freedom_of_the_World_2023-226] The 2024 [Bertelsmann Transformation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bertelsmann_Transformation_Index) ranked Albania 20th and acknowledged substantial progress in political and economic transformation.[^cite_note-Bertelsmann_Transformation_Index_2024-227] Also in 2024, the country was positioned 23rd in the [Global Gender Gap Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Gender_Gap_Report), excelling in the Economic Participation and Opportunity category with a global ranking of 16th, showcasing significant advancements in women's economic participation.[^cite_note-228]&#x20;

The economy, despite demonstrating considerable resilience, encounters substantial vulnerabilities that hinder its development.[^cite_note-ICS_US-229] Principal challenges include widespread corruption, distorted competition, and frequent modifications to fiscal legislation.[^cite_note-ICS_US-229] Furthermore, inadequate contract enforcement, a large informal sector, and persistent labor shortages further complicate the national economic progress.[^cite_note-Bertelsmann_Transformation_Index_2024-227][^cite_note-ICS_US-229] The economic landscape is characterised by regional disparities, particularly apparent in the concentration of economic activities in the triangular region formed by Tirana, Durrës and [Laç](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La%C3%A7).[^cite_note-230] This metropolitan area, referred to as Durana, functions as the principal economic center of Albania.[^cite_note-harvnb_unece_country-214][^cite_note-231] In the year 2021, Tirana County accounted for a significant 44% of the national GDP, with the counties of [Fier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fier_County) and [Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_County) also playing integral roles in the economy, contributing 11% and 10% to the GDP, respectively.[^cite_note-GDP_by_County-232] Other substantial economic centers, such as [Elbasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbasan) and the coastal cities of [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarand%C3%AB) and [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB), highlight the uneven distribution of economic development across the country.[^cite_note-harvnb_unece_country_1-233] Cultural regions, including [Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berat) and [Korçë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kor%C3%A7%C3%AB), despite their geographical distance from the primary economic hubs, have derived considerable benefits from the expansion of the tourism sector.[^cite_note-harvnb_unece_country_1-233]&#x20;

### Primary sector

Main article: [Agriculture in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Albania)



Grapes in [Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berat). Due to the [Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate), [wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_wine), [olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive) and [citrus fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citrus_fruit) are mostly produced in Southern Albania.

Albania produces significant amounts of fruits (apples, [olives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive), grapes, oranges, lemons, [apricots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apricot), [peaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peach), [cherries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherry), [figs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Figs), [sour cherries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prunus_cerasus), [plums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plum), and [strawberries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strawberry)), [vegetables](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vegetable_oil) (potatoes, tomatoes, [maize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize), onions, and wheat), [sugar beets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sugar_beet), tobacco, meat, [honey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honey), [dairy products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_product), traditional medicine and [aromatic plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essential_oil). The country is a worldwide significant producer of [salvia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvia), [rosemary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosemary) and [yellow gentian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gentiana_lutea).[^cite_note-234] The proximity to the Ionian Sea and the Adriatic Sea give the underdeveloped fishing industry great potential. The [World Bank](36752.md) and [European Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Community) economists report that the fishing industry has good potential to generate export earnings because prices in the nearby Greek and Italian markets are many times higher than those in the Albanian market. The seafood available off the coasts include [carp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carp), [trout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trout), [sea bream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_bream), [mussels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mussel) and [crustaceans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crustacean).&#x20;

Albania has one of Europe's longest histories of [viticulture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viticulture).[^cite_note-winealbania1-235] Today's region was one of the few places where vine was naturally grown during the ice age. The oldest found seeds in the region are 4,000 to 6,000 years old.[^cite_note-Stevenson2011-236] In 2009, the nation produced an estimated 17,500 tonnes of wine.[^cite_note-237]&#x20;

### Secondary sector

See also: [Category\:Mines in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Mines_in_Albania)



The [Antea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antea_Cement) factory in [Fushë-Krujë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fush%C3%AB-Kruj%C3%AB)

The secondary sector has undergone many changes and diversification since the communist regime collapsed. It is diversified, from electronics, manufacturing,[^cite_note-238] textiles, to food, cement, mining,[^cite_note-239] and energy. The [Antea Cement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antea_Cement) plant in Fushë-Krujë is considered one of the nation's largest industrial greenfield investments.[^cite_note-240] Oil and gas are promising, albeit strictly regulated, sectors, with the [second-largest oil deposits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves) in the Balkans and the largest [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patos-Marinza_Oil_Field)[^cite_note-241] in Europe. The [Albpetrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albpetrol) company is owned by the state and monitors the state petroleum agreements. The textile industry has seen an extensive expansion by approaching companies from the EU. According to the [Instituti i Statistikës (INSTAT)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Statistics_\(Albania\)), as of 2016, textile production had an annual growth of 5.3% and an annual turnover of around 1.5 billion euros.[^cite_note-242]&#x20;

Albania is a significant minerals producer and ranks among the world's leading [chromium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chromium) producers and exporters.[^cite_note-243] The nation is also a notable producer of copper, nickel, and coal.[^cite_note-244] The [Batra mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batra_mine), [Bulqizë mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulqiz%C3%AB_mine), and [Thekna mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thekna_mine) are among the most recognised mines still in operation.&#x20;

### Tertiary sector

See also: [Banking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banking_in_Albania), [Telecommunications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Albania), and [Tourism in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Albania)



The [Islets of Ksamil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islets_of_Ksamil), in the south of the [Albanian Ionian Sea Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Ionian_Sea_Coast)

The [tertiary sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector) represents the fastest growing sector. 36% of the population work in the service sector which contributes to 65% of the GDP.[^cite_note-DSSA-245] Since the end of the 20th century, the banking industry is a major component of the tertiary sector and remains in good conditions overall due to [privatisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Privatisation) and the commendable [monetary policy](297032.md).[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-DSSA-245]&#x20;

Previously one of the most [isolated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolationism) and controlled countries in the world, [telecommunication industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunication_industry) represents nowadays another major contributor to the sector. It developed largely through privatisation and subsequent investment by both domestic and foreign investors.[^cite_note-DSSA-245] [Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eagle_Mobile), [Vodafone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Albania) and [Telekom Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telekom_Albania) are the leading [telecommunications service providers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_service_provider).&#x20;

Tourism is recognised as an industry of national importance and has been steadily increasing since the beginning of the 21st century.[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] It directly accounted for 8.4% of GDP in 2016 though including indirect contributions pushes the proportion to 26%.[^cite_note-249] In the same year, the country received approximately 4.74 million visitors mostly from across Europe and the United States as well.[^cite_note-250]&#x20;

The increase of foreign visitors has been dramatic. Albania had 500,000 visitors in 2005 and an estimated 4.2 million in 2012, an increase of 740 percent. According to [UN Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Tourism), Albania welcomed approximately 11.7 million tourists in 2024, marking a significant increase from previous years.[^cite_note-251] The bulk of the tourist industry is concentrated along the Adriatic and Ionian Sea. The [Albanian Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Riviera) in the southwest has the most scenic and pristine beaches; its coastline has a considerable length of 446 kilometres (277 miles).[^cite_note-252] The coast has a distinctive character, rich in varieties of virgin beaches, capes, coves, covered bays, lagoons, small gravel beaches, sea caves, and many landforms. Some parts of this seaside are very clean ecologically, including unexplored areas, which are very rare within the Mediterranean.[^cite_note-253] Other attractions include the mountainous areas such as the Albanian Alps, Ceraunian Mountains and Korab Mountains but also the historical cities of [Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berat), Durrës, [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr), [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarand%C3%AB), Shkodër and Korçë.&#x20;

### Transport



[Rruga e Kombit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs-Kuk%C3%ABs_Highway) connects the [Adriatic Sea](23275478.md) across the [Western Lowlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Lowlands) with the [Albanian Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Alps).

[Transportation in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Albania) is managed within the functions of the [Ministry of Infrastructure and Energy](54109553.md) and entities such as the [Albanian Road Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Road_Authority), responsible for the construction and maintenance of the [highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highways_in_Albania) and [motorways in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorways_in_Albania), as well as the [Albanian Aviation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Aviation_Authority_\(Albania\)), with the responsibility of coordinating civil aviation and airports.&#x20;

The [Tirana International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana_International_Airport_N%C3%ABn%C3%AB_Tereza) is the premier air gateway to the country and is the principal hub for the national [flag carrier](465816.md) airline, [Air Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Albania). The airport carried more than 3.3 million passengers in 2019 with connections to many destinations in other countries around Europe, Africa and Asia.[^cite_note-254] The country plans to progressively increase the number of airports especially in the south with possible locations in [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarand%C3%AB), Gjirokastër and [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlor%C3%AB_International_Airport).[^cite_note-255]&#x20;

The [Autostrada 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rruga_e_Kombit) (A1) is an integral transportation corridor and the country's longest motorway. It is planned to link Durrës to [Pristina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pristina), Kosovo with the [Pan-European Corridor X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_Corridor_X) in Serbia.[^cite_note-SEETO-256][^cite_note-257] The [Autostrada 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A2_\(Albania\)) (A2) is part of the [Adriatic–Ionian Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic%E2%80%93Ionian_motorway) as well as the [Pan-European Corridor VIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_Corridor_VIII) and connects [Fier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fier) with Vlorë.[^cite_note-SEETO-256] The [Autostrada 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A3_\(Albania\)) (A3) is under construction and after its completion will connect [Tirana](31075.md) and [Elbasan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbasan) with the Pan-European Corridor VIII. When all three corridors are completed, Albania will have an estimated 759 kilometres (472 mi) of highway, linking it with all neighboring countries.&#x20;

The [Port of Durrës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Durr%C3%ABs) is the busiest and largest seaport, followed by [Vlorë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Vlor%C3%AB), [Shëngjin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Sh%C3%ABngjin) and [Sarandë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Sarand%C3%AB). The rail network is administered by the national railway company [Hekurudha Shqiptare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hekurudha_Shqiptare). A new railway line from Tirana and its airport to Durrës is planned. The location of this railway, connecting the most populated urban areas, makes it an important economic development project.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259]&#x20;

### Energy

Main article: [Renewable energy in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Albania)

See also: [Power stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_Albania) and [Oil fields in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oil_and_gas_fields_in_Albania)



Electricity production in Albania from 1980 to 2019

Albania has a wide variety of energy resources, ranging from gas, oil, and coal to wind, solar, water, and other renewable sources.[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] According to the [World Economic Forum](172185.md)'s 2023 Energy Transition Index, the country ranked 21st globally, highlighting the progress in its energy transition agenda.[^cite_note-262] The electricity generation sector depends on [hydroelectricity](381399.md), ranking fifth in the world.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] Increasing risks of river floods and droughts due to climate change is expected to put electricity generation at risk.[^cite_note-imfcountryreport-175] The Drin hosts four hydroelectric power stations, including [Fierza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fierza_Hydroelectric_Power_Station), [Koman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koman_Hydroelectric_Power_Station), [Skavica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skavica_Hydro_Power_Plant) and [Vau i Dejës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vau_i_Dej%C3%ABs_Hydroelectric_Power_Station). Two other power stations, such as the [Banjë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banj%C3%AB_Hydro_Power_Plant) and [Moglicë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moglic%C3%AB_Hydro_Power_Plant), are along the [Devoll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devoll_\(river\)) in the south.[^cite_note-266]&#x20;

The main petroleum deposits are located around the Adriatic Sea Coast and [Myzeqe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myzeqe) Plain within the Western Lowlands, where the largest reserve is located. [Patos-Marinza Oil Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patos-Marinza_Oil_Field) is the largest onshore oil field in Europe.[^cite_note-267] The [Trans Adriatic Pipeline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans_Adriatic_Pipeline), part of the planned [Southern Gas Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Gas_Corridor), runs for 215 kilometres (134 miles) across Albania before entering the Adriatic Sea Coast approximately 17 kilometres (11 miles) northwest of [Fier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fier).[^cite_note-268]&#x20;

Water resources are particularly abundant in all regions and comprise [lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_lakes_of_Albania), [rivers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rivers_of_Albania), springs, and groundwater aquifers.[^cite_note-269] The available average quantity of fresh water is estimated at 129.7 cubic metres (4,580 cubic feet) per inhabitant per year, one of the highest rates in Europe.[^cite_note-270] According to data presented by the [Joint Monitoring Programme for Water Supply and Sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Monitoring_Programme_for_Water_Supply_and_Sanitation) in 2015, about 93% of the population had access to [improved sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Improved_sanitation).[^cite_note-271]&#x20;

### Media

Main article: [Media in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Albania)



The former grounds of the headquarters of [Radio Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Tirana) in the capital of [Tirana](31075.md). [Radio Televizioni Shqiptar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Televizioni_Shqiptar) (RTSH) was initially inaugurated as Radio Tirana in 1938 prior to [World War II](32927.md).

The freedom of press and speech and the right to free expression are guaranteed in the constitution.[^cite_note-272] Albania was ranked 84th on the [Press Freedom Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index) of 2020 compiled by the [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md), with its score steadily declining since 2003.[^cite_note-273] Nevertheless, in the 2020 report of [Freedom in the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World), the [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House) classified the freedoms of press and speech as partly free from political interference and manipulation.[^cite_note-274]&#x20;

[Radio Televizioni Shqiptar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Televizioni_Shqiptar) is the [national broadcaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_broadcaster) corporation operating numerous television and radio stations.[^cite_note-275] The three major private broadcaster corporations are [Top Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Top_Channel), [Televizioni Klan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV_Klan) and [Vizion Plus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vizion_Plus) whose content are distributed throughout Albania and beyond its territory in Kosovo and other Albanian-speaking territories.&#x20;

[Albanian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Albania) has its roots in the 20th century and developed after its declaration of independence.[^cite_note-Cinema-276] The first movie theater exclusively devoted to showing motion pictures was built in 1912 in Shkodër.[^cite_note-Cinema-276] During the People's Republic of Albania, cinema developed rapidly with the inauguration of the [Kinostudio Shqipëria e Re](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kinostudio_Shqip%C3%ABria_e_Re) in Tirana.[^cite_note-Cinema-276] In 1953, the Albanian-Soviet [epic film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_film) *[The Great Warrior Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Great_Warrior_Skanderbeg)* was released chronicling the life and fight of the medieval hero Skanderbeg. It went on to win the international prize at the [1954 Cannes Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Cannes_Film_Festival). In 2003, the [Tirana International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tirana_International_Film_Festival) was established. The [Durrës Amphitheatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_Amphitheatre) is host to the [Durrës International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_International_Film_Festival).&#x20;

### Technology

Main articles: [Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Albania) and [Telecommunications in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_in_Albania)

After the fall of communism in 1991, human resources in sciences and technology have drastically decreased. As of various reports, during 1991 to 2005 approximately 50% of the professors and scientists of the universities and science institutions have left Albania.[^cite_note-dfid-277] Albania was ranked 67th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280]&#x20;

Telecommunication represents one of the fastest growing and dynamic sectors.[^cite_note-National_Plan-281][^cite_note-DSSA-245] [Vodafone Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodafone_Albania), [Telekom Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telekom_Albania) and [Albtelecom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albtelecom) are the three large providers of mobile and internet.[^cite_note-National_Plan-281] According to the [Electronic and Postal Communications Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_and_Postal_Communications_Authority_\(Albania\)) in 2018, the country had approximately 2.7 million active mobile users with almost 1.8 million active broadband subscribers.[^cite_note-AKEP-282] Vodafone Albania served more than 931,000 mobile users, Telekom Albania had about 605,000 users, and Albtelecom had more than 272,000 users.[^cite_note-AKEP-282] In [January 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_spaceflight_launches_in_January%E2%80%93June_2023), Albania launched its [first two satellites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_first_satellites_by_country), *Albania 1* and *Albania 2*, into orbit in what was regarded as a milestone effort in monitoring the country's territory and identifying illegal activities.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] Albanian-American engineer [Mira Murati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mira_Murati), the Chief Technology Officer of research organisation [OpenAI](48795986.md), played a substantial role in the development and launch of artificial intelligence services such as [ChatGPT](72417803.md), [Codex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenAI_Codex_\(AI_agent\)) and [DALL-E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DALL-E).[^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286] In December 2023, Prime Minister Edi Rama announced plans for collaboration between the Albanian government and ChatGPT, facilitated by discussions with Murati.[^cite_note-Taylor-287][^cite_note-288] Rama emphasised the intention to streamline the alignment of Albanian laws with the regulations of the European Union, aiming to reduce costs associated with translation and legal services.[^cite_note-Taylor-287]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demography of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Albania)

See also: [Albanian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_people) and [Origin of the Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Origin_of_the_Albanians)



Development of the [population of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Albania) over the last sixty years

The 2023 census conducted by the [Instituti i Statistikave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Statistics_\(Albania\)) indicated a population of 2,402,113, with a notable decline from the 2,821,977 recorded in the 2011 census.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024105-289][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2011_Albanian_census20127-290] The decrease in inhabitants began after the disintegration of the communist regime and is associated with significant shifts within the political, economic, and social structure.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPopulation_Dynamics201425-291][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024114-292] A principal factor in this transition incorporates a decline in fertility rates coupled with an increase in emigration, both contributing to persistent demographic changes and challenges.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPopulation_Dynamics201439-293] It is forecast that the population will continue shrinking for the next decade at least, depending on the actual rates and the level of migration.[^cite_note-294] The population density is 83.6 inhabitants per square kilometer with a varied distribution of inhabitants across different regions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024105-289][^cite_note-295] The counties of Tirana and Durrës showcase substantial concentrations of people, accounting for about 41% of the overall demographic, with 32% residing in Tirana and 9% in Durrës.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024117-296] Conversely, more peripheral and rural counties such as [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr_County) and [Kukës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuk%C3%ABs_County) present significantly lower population densities, with each aiding 3% to the overall population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024117-296]&#x20;

Historically, the Albanian people have established several communities in many regions throughout Southern Europe. The [Albanian diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_diaspora) has been formed since the late Middle Ages, when they emigrated to escape either various socio-political difficulties or the Ottoman conquest.[^cite_note-297] Following the fall of communism, large numbers of Albanians have migrated to countries such as Australia, Canada, Switzerland, the United Kingdom and the United States. Albanian minorities are present in the neighbouring territories such as the west of North Macedonia, the southeast of Montenegro, Kosovo in its entirety and parts of southern Serbia. Altogether, the number of ethnic Albanians living abroad is estimated to be higher than the total population inside Albania. As much as a third of those born in the country's borders now live outside of it, making Albania one of the countries with the highest rate of outmigration relative to its population in the world.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] In 2022 the birth rate was 20% lower than in 2021, largely due to emigration of people of childbearing age.[^cite_note-300]&#x20;

### Urbanisation

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of cities and towns in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Albania).

Subsequent to the collapse of communism in 1991, Albania has undergone a remarkable transformation in its urban landscape, emerging as one of the fastest urbanising countries in Europe.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternal_Migration_Albania201412-301][^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303] At the forefront of this transformation is the Tirana–Durrës agglomeration, a densely populated urban corridor along the west coast.[^cite_note-CIEACA_2-304] This corridor has become the primary locus of population growth and settlement development, attracting a significant influx of internal migrants from peripheral areas.[^cite_note-CIEACA_2-304] Despite an overall decline of total population, the proportion of the urban demographic has consistently progressed from 47% in 2001 to 65% in 2023.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024114-292][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2011_Albanian_census201210-305][^cite_note-306] This sustained increase, coupled with the concentration in the Tirana-Durrës region, has led to a spread of regional imbalances, with the peripheral areas, particularly [Dibër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dib%C3%ABr_County) and [Kukës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuk%C3%ABs_County), experiencing severe depopulation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternal_Migration_Albania201412_&_18-307][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternal_Migration_Albania201415-308][^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census2024117-296]&#x20;

### Language

Main article: [Languages of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Albania)

See also: [Albanian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language)

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(May 2023)* |
| --- | --- |

Languages spoken at home as of 2023:

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202477-321]

1. Albanian (91.1%)

2. Other language (1.54%)

3. Multiple languages (0.57%)

4. No answer (1.21%)

5. Unavailable (5.59%)

The [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) of the country is [Albanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_language) which is spoken by the vast majority.[^cite_note-Kushtetuta-183] Its standard spoken and written form is revised and merged from the two main dialects, [Gheg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gheg) and [Tosk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tosk_Albanian), though it is notably based more on the Tosk dialect. The Shkumbin river is the rough dividing line between the two dialects. Among minority languages, Greek is the second most-spoken language in the country, with 0.5 to 3% of the population speaking it as first language, mainly in the country's south where its speakers are concentrated.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-SecondMostSpokenLanguage-324][^cite_note-kathimerini-325] Other languages spoken by ethnic minorities in Albania include [Aromanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aromanian_language), [Serbo-Croatian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbo-Croatian), [Macedonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonian_language), [Bulgarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgarian_language), [Gorani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gora_dialect), and [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_language).[^cite_note-326] Macedonian is official in the Pustec Municipality in East Albania. In the 2023 population census, 91.07% reported Albanian as the language spoken at home. 1.54% declared to speak at home another language, 0.57% multiple languages, 1.21% gave no answer and 5.59% were unavailable.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202477-321]&#x20;

Among young people aged 25 or less, English, German and Turkish have seen rising interest after 2000. Italian and French have had a stable interest, while Greek has lost much of its previous interest. The trends are linked with cultural and economic factors.[^cite_note-Shkurtaj2017-327]&#x20;

In the mid-2010s, an increasing number of Albanians showed interest in learning the German language,[^cite_note-328] which has been explained as part of "a Western Balkan trend of preparing to integrate into the German labor market".[^cite_note-329] Some of them have gone to Germany for studies or work. Albania and Germany have agreements for cooperating in helping young people of the two countries know both cultures better.[^cite_note-330] Due to a sharp rise in economic relations with Turkey, interest in learning Turkish, in particular among young people, was also growing on a yearly basis in the 2010s.[^cite_note-331]&#x20;

### Minorities

Ethnic groups as of 2023:

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202475-332]

1. Albanians (91.0%)

2. Greeks (0.98%)

3. Balkan Egyptians (0.50%)

4. Roma (0.40%)

5. Bulgarians (0.29%)

6. Bosnians (0.12%)

7. Aromanians (0.10%)

8. Macedonians (0.09%)

9. Serbs (0.02%)

10. Montenegrins (0.02%)

11. Mixed ethnicity (0.03%)

12. Other ethnicity (0.15%)

13. Unspecified (6.20%)

Designated national and cultural [minorities in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minorities_of_Albania) include Aromanian, Greek, Macedonian, Montenegrin, Serb, Roma, Egyptian, Bosnian and Bulgarian peoples, as well as Gorani and Jews as other minority groups.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] The 2023 census recorded the ethnic composition as [Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanians) 2,186,917 (91,04% of the total), [Greeks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeks_in_Albania) 23,485 (0,98%), [Macedonians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macedonians_of_Albania) 2,281 (0,09%), [Montenegrins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montenegrins_of_Albania) 511 (0,02%), [Aromanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aromanians_in_Albania) 2,459 (0,1%), [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people_in_Albania) 9,813 (0.4%), [Balkan Egyptians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balkan_Egyptians) 12,375 (0,5%), [Bosnians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosniaks_in_Albania) 2,963 (0,12%), [Serbians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbs_and_Montenegrins_in_Albania) 584 (0,02%), [Bulgarians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgarians_in_Albania) 7,057 (0,29%), mixed ethnicities 770 (0.03%), other ethnicities 3,798 (0.15%), and 134,451 (5.60%) with unspecified ethnicity, out of a total population of 2,402,113.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202475-332]&#x20;

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Albania)

[Religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Albania) as of 2023:

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202476-4]

1. [Islam](6037917.md) (45.9%)

2. [Bektashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bektashi) (4.81%)

3. [Roman Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism) (8.38%)

4. [Eastern Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy) (7.22%)

5. [Evangelicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) (0.40%)

6. Non-affiliated believers (13.8%)

7. [Atheism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism) (3.55%)

8. Undeclared (15.8%)

9. Other (0.15%)

Albania is a secular and religiously diverse country with no [official religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_religion). [Freedom of religion](46006.md), [belief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_belief) and [conscience](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_conscience) are guaranteed under the constitution.[^cite_note-Kushtetuta-183] As of the 2023 census, there were 1,101,718 (45.86%) [Sunni Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam), 201,530 (8.38%) [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism), 173,645 (7.22%) [Eastern Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy), 115,644 (4.81%) [Bektashi Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bektashi_Order), 9,658 (0.4%) [Evangelicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism), 3,670 (0.15%) of other religions, 332,155 (13.82%) believers without religion or denomination, 85,311 (3.55%) Atheists and 378,782 (15.76%) did not provide an answer.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2023_Albanian_census202476-4] Albania is nevertheless ranked among the least religious countries in the world.[^cite_note-335] Religion constitute an important role in the lives of only 39% of the population.[^cite_note-336] In another report, 56% considered themselves religious, 30% considered themselves non-religious, while 9% defined themselves as convinced atheists. 80% believed in God.[^cite_note-337]&#x20;

The [Muslim Albanians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Albania) are spread throughout the country. [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodoxy_in_Albania) and [Bektashis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bektashism) are mostly found in the south, whereas [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholicism_in_Albania) mainly live in the north.[^cite_note-International_Religious_Freedom_Report_2007-338] In 2008, there were 694 Catholic churches and 425 [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_Orthodox_Church) churches, 568 mosques and 70 bektashi [takyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takya) in the country.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340]&#x20;

During modern times, the republican, monarchic and later communist regimes followed a systematic policy of separating religion from official functions and cultural life. The country has never had an [official religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_religion) either as a republic or as a kingdom. In the 20th century, the clergy of all faiths was weakened under the monarchy and ultimately eradicated during the 1950s and 1960s, under the state policy of obliterating all organised religion from the territories of Albania. The communist regime persecuted and suppressed religious observance and institutions and entirely banned religion. The country was then officially declared to be the world's first [atheist state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheist_state). Religious freedom has returned, however, since the end of communism.&#x20;

Islam survived communist era persecution and reemerged in the modern era as a practised religion.[^cite_note-Merdjanova673940-341] Some smaller Christian sects include [Evangelicals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) and several [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) communities including [Seventh-day Adventist Church](28632.md), [Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints) and [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses).[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] The first recorded Protestant of Albania was Said Toptani, who travelled around Europe and returned to Tirana in 1853, where he preached [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism). The first [evangelical Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical_Protestants) appeared in the 19th century and the Evangelical Alliance was founded in 1892. Nowadays, it has 160 member congregations from different Protestant denominations. Following mass emigration to Israel after the fall of communism, there are only 200 [Jews in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Albania).[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347]&#x20;

### Education

Main article: [Education in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Albania)



The [University of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Arts_\(Albania\)) is the largest higher education institute dedicated to the study of arts.

Education is secular, free, [compulsory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education), and based on three levels.[^cite_note-Official-348][^cite_note-Epnuffic-349] The academic year is apportioned into two semesters, beginning in September or October and ending in June or July. Albanian is the primary language of instruction in the country's academic institutions.[^cite_note-Epnuffic-349] The study of a first foreign language is mandatory and taught most often at elementary and bilingual schools.[^cite_note-foreign_languages-350] Languages taught in schools are English, Italian, French and German.[^cite_note-foreign_languages-350] The [school life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_life_expectancy) is 16 years and literacy rate is 98.7%, with 99.2% for men and 98.3% for women.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-cia_fb2013-352]&#x20;

Compulsory primary education is divided into two levels, elementary and secondary school, from grade one to five and six to nine, respectively.[^cite_note-Official-348] Pupils are required to attend school from the age six until they turn 16. Upon successful completion of primary education, all pupils are entitled to attend high schools, specialising in any field, including arts, sports, languages, sciences, and technology.[^cite_note-Official-348]&#x20;

Tertiary education is optional and has undergone a thorough reformation and restructuring in compliance with the principles of the [Bologna Process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bologna_Process). There are a significant number of private and public institutions of higher education in the major cities.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-Epnuffic-349] Tertiary education is organised into three successive levels, the [bachelor](188874.md), [master](170346.md), and [doctorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctorate_Degree).&#x20;

### Health

Main article: [Health in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Albania)

See also: [Healthcare in Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Albania)



The [Albanian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_cuisine) from the Mediterranean, which is characterised by the use of fruits, vegetables and [olive oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive_oil), contributes to the good nutrition of the country's population.

[^cite_note-354]

The constitution guarantees its citizens equal, free, and [universal health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_health_care).[^cite_note-Kushtetuta-183] The health care system is organised into [primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_healthcare), [secondary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_care), and [tertiary healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_healthcare), and is in a process of modernisation and development.[^cite_note-cod-355][^cite_note-hospitals-356] The [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) at birth is 77.8 years, ranking [37th](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy) in the world and surpassing several developed countries.[^cite_note-357] The average [healthy life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy#Healthy_life_expectancy) is 68.8 years, ranking 37th in the world.[^cite_note-358] The [infant mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate) was estimated at 12 per 1,000 live births in 2015. In 2000, the country had the world's 55th-best healthcare performance, as defined by the [World Health Organisation](33583.md).[^cite_note-359]&#x20;

Cardiovascular disease is the principal cause of death, accounting for 52% of deaths.[^cite_note-cod-355] [Accidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accident), injuries, [malignant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malignant_disease) and [respiratory diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_disease) are other primary causes of death.[^cite_note-cod-355] [Neuropsychiatric disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuropsychiatric_disorders) has also increased due to recent demographic, social, and economic changes.[^cite_note-cod-355]&#x20;

In 2009, Albania had a fruit and vegetable supply of 886 grams per capita per day, the fifth-highest supply in Europe.[^cite_note-360] Compared to other developed and developing countries, Albania has a relatively low rate of [obesity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obesity), probably thanks to the [Mediterranean diet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_diet).[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362] According to [2016 WHO data](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Body_Mass_Index_\(BMI\)#WHO_Data_on_Prevalence_of_Obesity_\(BMI_above_30\)_\(2014\)), 21.7% of adults in the country are clinically [overweight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overweight), with a [Body mass index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Body_mass_index) (BMI) score of 25 or more.[^cite_note-363]&#x20;

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Albania)

### Symbols

Main article: [National symbols of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Albania)



The [helmet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_of_Skanderbeg) of [Gjergj Kastrioti Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg) stands as an emblem of Albanian independence.

The red and black [national colours](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_colours) are displayed on the [flag of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Albania) that is adorned with a [double-headed eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double-headed_eagle) as the foremost recognised [national symbol of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Albania).[^cite_note-Flamuri-364][^cite_note-Flag_Elsie-365][^cite_note-Minahan-366] With the black eagle portraying bravery and strength, the red field symbolises the fortitude and sacrifices of the Albanian people.[^cite_note-Flamuri-364] The eagle is linked with the legacy of the national hero Skanderbeg.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368][^cite_note-Minahan-366] Rooted in the Middle Ages, it emerged as a [heraldic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_heraldry) [symbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armorial_of_Albania) in the Principality of Arbër and among notable Albanian dynasties such as the [Dukagjini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dukagjini_family), [Kastrioti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kastrioti_family), [Muzaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muzaka_family) and [Thopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thopia_family) clans.[^cite_note-369] Amid the Albanian Renaissance, marking the resurgence of Albanian national identity and aspirations for independence, the Albanian eagle regained its prominence.[^cite_note-Flag_Elsie-365] Its importance reached its culmination with Albania's declaration of independence in 1912, when [Ismail Qemali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Qemali) raised it as the national flag in Vlorë.[^cite_note-Flag_Elsie-365]&#x20;

The [coat of arms of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Albania) is an adaptation of the flag of Albania and the seal of Skanderbeg.[^cite_note-Flamuri-364] The coat comprises the black double-headed eagle positioned at the centre of a red field.[^cite_note-Flamuri-364] Above the eagle, it carries the [helmet of Skanderbeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arms_of_Skanderbeg) that is crowned with the head of a golden horned goat.[^cite_note-Minahan-366] [Ti Shqipëri, më jep nder, më jep emrin Shqipëtar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ti_Shqip%C3%ABri,_m%C3%AB_jep_nder,_m%C3%AB_jep_emrin_Shqip%C3%ABtar), which translates to "You Albania, give me honour, give me the name Albanian", is the [national motto of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_mottos).[^cite_note-Flag_Elsie-365][^cite_note-Minahan-366] It finds its foundation in the legacy of [national poet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_poet) [Naim Frashëri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naim_Frash%C3%ABri), who held a renowned role during the Albanian Renaissance.[^cite_note-Flag_Elsie-365] The [national anthem of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_anthem), "[Himni i Flamurit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himni_i_Flamurit)", was composed by [Asdreni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksand%C3%ABr_Stavre_Drenova) and adopted as such following Albania's independence in 1912.[^cite_note-Flamuri-364]&#x20;

### Clothing



The [Fustanella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fustanella) is designated as the [national costume of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_costume_of_Albania) with profound cultural significance within Albanian culture.

[Albanian traditional clothing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditional_Albanian_clothing), with its contrasting variations for the northern Gheg and southern Tosk Albanians, is a testament to Albania's history, cultural diversity and ethnic identity.[^cite_note-Gjergji-370] Gheg men wear a light-colored shirt paired with wide loose-fitting trousers referred to as the Tirq. These trousers are securely fastened by a wide woven belt, the Brez. An integral component of their costume is the [xhamadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhamadan), a woolen red velvet vest adorned with folkloric motifs and gold patterns.[^cite_note-371] They also wear a distinctive dome-shaped hat known as the [qeleshe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qeleshe), crafted from wool, with its origins tracing back to the Illyrians.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373] Conversely, Tosk men opt for the [fustanella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fustanella), a knee-length garment designated as the national costume, typically white with pleats, accompanied by a white shirt. They also wear a beige or dark blue xhamadan reminiscent of the Gheg attire. To complete their attire, men wear knee-high socks referred to as [çorape](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%87orape) and leather shoes known as [opinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinga).&#x20;

The attire of Gheg and Tosk women is known for its rich palette of colors and intricate filigree detailing, displayed across various components including ear ornaments, finger rings, necklaces, belt buckles and buttons. Key components include a long, light-toned shirt paired with an apron, often accompanied by a woolen or felt mantle referred to as the xhoka. Additional upper garments, such as the dollama or mintan, may be layered, along with a headscarf known as kapica or shall. A distinctive variation incorporates a pair of aprons, including the pështjellak at the rear and the paranik at the front. Integral to the traditional attire of Gheg women is the ancient [xhubleta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xhubleta).[^cite_note-Gjergji-370] Dedicated efforts have been undertaken to preserve and promote the dress, acknowledging its significance as an emblem of Albanian heritage.[^cite_note-Xhubleta-374] As a testament to its importance, the xhubleta has been inscribed on the [UNESCO](21786641.md) [Intangible Cultural Heritage List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists).[^cite_note-Xhubleta-374] The distinctive bell-shaped garment is fashioned in black and enriched with embroidered ethnic Albanian motifs, showcasing the remarkable craftsmanship of northern Albania.[^cite_note-Gjergji-370] Its crafting process involves multiple intricate stages, encompassing the preparation of shajak fabric and the precise cutting techniques.[^cite_note-Xhubleta-374]&#x20;

### Art and architecture



[Butrint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint) has been included in the [UNESCO](21786641.md) list of [World Heritage Sites](44940.md) since 1992.



*d[Motra Tone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motra_Tone)* by [Kolë Idromeno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kol%C3%AB_Idromeno).

The [artistic history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_art) of Albania has been particularly influenced by a multitude of ancient and medieval people, traditions and religions. It covers a broad spectrum with mediums and disciplines that include painting, pottery, sculpture, ceramics and architecture all of them exemplifying a great variety in style and shape, in different regions and period.&#x20;

The rise of the Byzantine and Ottoman Empire in the Middle Ages was accompanied by a corresponding growth in [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_art) and [Islamic art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_art) in the lands of Albania which are apparent in examples of architecture and mosaics throughout the country.[^cite_note-www\.albanianart.net_index_al-375] Centuries later, the Albanian Renaissance proved crucial to the emancipation of the modern Albanian culture and saw unprecedented developments in all fields of literature and art whereas artists sought to return to the ideals of [Impressionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impressionism) and [Romanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism_\(art\)).[^cite_note-376] Artists include [Onufri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onufri), [Kolë Idromeno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kol%C3%AB_Idromeno), [David Selenica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Selenica), [Kostandin Shpataraku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kostandin_Shpataraku) and the [Zografi Brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zografi_Brothers).&#x20;

The [architecture of Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Albania) reflects the legacy of various civilisations tracing back to the [classical antiquity](252905.md). Major cities have evolved from within the castle to include dwellings, religious and commercial structures, with constant redesigning of town squares and evolution of building techniques. Nowadays, the cities and towns reflect a whole spectrum of various architectural styles. In the 20th century, many historical as well as [sacred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_architecture) buildings bearing the ancient influence were demolished during the communist era.[^cite_note-377]&#x20;

[Ancient architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_architecture) is found throughout Albania and most visible in [Byllis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byllis), [Amantia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amantia), [Phoenice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenice), Apollonia, [Butrint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butrint), [Antigonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antigonia_\(Chaonia\)), Shkodër and Durrës. Considering the long period of rule of the Byzantine Empire, they introduced castles, citadels, churches and monasteries with spectacular wealth of visible [murals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural) and [frescos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresco). Perhaps the best known examples can be found in the southern Albanian cities and surroundings of [Korçë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kor%C3%A7%C3%AB), [Berat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berat), [Voskopojë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voskopoj%C3%AB) and [Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gjirokast%C3%ABr). Involving the introduction of [Ottoman architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_architecture) there was a development of mosques and other Islamic buildings, particularly seen in Berat and Gjirokastër.&#x20;

A productive period of [Historicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historicism_\(art\)), [Art Nouveau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Nouveau) and [Neoclassicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassicism) merged into the 19th century, best exemplified in Korçë. The 20th century brought new architectural styles such as the modern [Italian style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italianate_architecture), which is present in Tirana such as the [Skanderbeg Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skanderbeg_Square) and Ministries. It is also present in Shkodër, Vlorë, Sarandë and Durrës. Moreover, other towns received their present-day Albania-unique appearance through various cultural or economic influences.&#x20;

[Socialist classicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_classicism) arrived during the communist era. Many socialist-styled complexes, wide roads and factories were constructed, while town squares were redesigned and numerous of historic and important buildings demolished. Notable examples of that style include the [Mother Teresa Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Teresa_Square_\(Tirana\)), [Pyramid of Tirana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyramid_of_Tirana), and [Palace of Congresses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Congresses).&#x20;

Three archaeological sites are included in the list of [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](44940.md). These include the ancient remains of Butrint, the medieval [Historic Centres of Berat and Gjirokastër](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Centres_of_Berat_and_Gjirokast%C3%ABr), and [Natural and Cultural Heritage of the Ohrid region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohrid_Lake) site shared with [North Macedonia](23564616.md) since 2019.[^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] Furthermore, the royal [Illyrian tombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Tombs_of_Selca_e_Poshtme), the remains of Apollonia, the ancient [Amphitheatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durr%C3%ABs_Amphitheatre) of Durrës and the [Fortress of Bashtovë](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortress_of_Bashtov%C3%AB) has been included on the tentative list.&#x20;

### Music

See also: [Albanian war dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_war_dances)



[Albanian iso-polyphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_iso-polyphony) is designated as an [UNESCO](21786641.md) [Masterpiece of the Oral and Intangible Heritage of Humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masterpieces_of_the_Oral_and_Intangible_Heritage_of_Humanity).

[^cite_note-380]

Albanian folk music is a prominent part of the national identity, and continues to play a major part in overall [Albanian music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Albania). Folk music can be divided into two stylistic groups, mainly the northern Gheg varieties, and southern Lab and Tosk varieties. Northern and southern traditions are contrasted by a rugged tone from the north, and the more relaxed southern form of music.&#x20;

Many songs concern events from Albanian history and culture, including traditional themes of honour, hospitality, treachery, and revenge. The first compilation of folk music was made by two [Himariot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Himara) musicians, [Neço Muka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ne%C3%A7o_Muko) and Koço Çakali, in Paris, during their work with Albanian soprano [Tefta Tashko-Koço](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tefta_Tashko-Ko%C3%A7o). Several [gramophone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phonograph_record) compilations were recorded at the time by the three artists, which eventually led to the recognition of [Albanian iso-polyphony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_iso-polyphony) as a [UNESCO](21786641.md) [Intangible Cultural Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_Lists).[^cite_note-381]&#x20;

[Festivali i Këngës](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festivali_i_K%C3%ABng%C3%ABs) is a traditional song contest organised by the national broadcaster [Radio Televizioni Shqiptar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_Televizioni_Shqiptar). The festival is celebrated annually since its inauguration in 1962 and has launched the careers of some of Albania's most successful singers including [Vaçe Zela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Va%C3%A7e_Zela) and [Parashqevi Simaku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parashqevi_Simaku).[^cite_note-382] It is significantly a music competition among performers presenting unreleased songs in premiere, composed by Albanian authors and voted by juries or by public.&#x20;

Contemporary artists [Rita Ora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rita_Ora), [Bebe Rexha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bebe_Rexha), [Era Istrefi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Era_Istrefi), [Dua Lipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dua_Lipa), [Ava Max](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ava_Max), [Bleona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bleona), [Elvana Gjata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elvana_Gjata), [Ermonela Jaho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ermonela_Jaho), and [Inva Mula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inva_Mula) have achieved international recognition for their music,[^cite_note-383] while soprano [Ermonela Jaho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ermonela_Jaho) has been described by some as the "world's most acclaimed soprano".[^cite_note-384] Opera singer [Saimir Pirgu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saimir_Pirgu) was nominated for the 2017 [Grammy Award](44635.md).[^cite_note-385]&#x20;

### Literature

Main article: [Albanian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albanian_literature)

Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence). *(October 2018)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
